- **★ Vikings Bạch Đằng** (Vikings Bạch Đằng (Vikings Cyber)) · Bạch Đằng riverside, east of the Old Quarter (Hoàn Kiếm) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - The closest esports-grade cyber to the Old Quarter has 217 PCs on the Bạch Đằng riverside, every one on a 240–390 Hz screen. It is run by Vikings, the Hanoi chain behind the pro team MVK Esports.
  - Tip: For CS2 or Valorant, take the Competitive Zone (Ryzen 7 9700X, RTX 4060 Ti, ASUS ROG PG259QN 360 Hz, 18,900 VND/h), or the Master and Duo zones with 390 Hz Acer panels (16,900/h). Staff create a Vikings ID at the cashier, and new accounts get 10 free minutes. Book machines on the hotline 090 224 2247 or through Vikings' Facebook or Zalo. The other central branch is Vikings Net at 15 Đoàn Trần Nghiệp, near Vincom Bà Triệu, which has a Team Room with RTX 4070 Ti PCs and 360 Hz screens.
  - Address: 64 Bạch Đằng, Hồng Hà ward, Hà Nội
  - Price: 10,500 VND/h (Standard: RTX 3050, 240 Hz) to 24,900 (Stream Room); Super Zone 14,500, Master and Duo 16,900, Competitive and Private 18,900 (official page, Oct 2026)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Vikings%20B%E1%BA%A1ch%20%C4%90%E1%BA%B1ng%2C%2064%20B%E1%BA%A1ch%20%C4%90%E1%BA%B1ng%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://www.vikings.vn/cyber/bach-dang)
- **Vikings Hoàng Hoa Thám** · Ba Đình (Hoàng Hoa Thám, Ngọc Hà ward) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - Vikings' big Ba Đình branch has 250 PCs over three floors and the chain's newest kit, including a five-seat Team Room with Ryzen 7 7800X3D, RTX 5060 Ti and Zowie XL2566X+ monitors. Between matches you can use its café, which has panoramic city views toward West Lake, and its retro 4- and 6-button arcade cabinets.
  - Tip: Bring four friends and book the Team Room (21,900 VND/h per seat) for a CS2 or Valorant five-stack. Solo, take the Competitive Zone (7800X3D, RTX 4060 Ti, Zowie XL2566K, 18,900/h) or a Private Zone seat with an RTX 5060 and a 27-inch 320 Hz screen (18,900/h). The hotline is 079 400 2247. Each Vikings branch keeps its own balance, so top up where you play.
  - Address: Floors 5–7, 713 Hoàng Hoa Thám, Ngọc Hà ward, Hà Nội
  - Price: 12,500 VND/h (Pro Zone, RTX 2060 Super) to 24,900 (Stream Room); Team Room 21,900 (official page, Oct 2026)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Vikings%20Ho%C3%A0ng%20Hoa%20Th%C3%A1m%2C%20713%20Ho%C3%A0ng%20Hoa%20Th%C3%A1m%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://www.vikings.vn/cyber/hoang-hoa-tham)
- **OEG Cyber Hub Lạc Long Quân** (OEG Cyber Hub 318 Lạc Long Quân) · West Lake – Lạc Long Quân (Tây Hồ) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - The handiest high-spec cyber for a West Lake stay is a compact 92-PC 'Net Mart' over four floors. It has RTX 50-series PCs, 420 Hz AOC monitors and a mini-mart downstairs for snacks and drinks.
  - Tip: Hyper Space (i7-12700KF, RTX 5060, 420 Hz, 23,100 VND/h) is the shooter sweet spot. Private rooms pair a Ryzen 7 7800X3D with an RTX 5060 (27,500/h). The hotline is 079 645 9318. If it's full, OEG has more Cyber Hubs at 178 Tây Sơn, 2 Trần Vỹ, 175 Trần Quốc Hoàn and 101 Chiến Thắng.
  - Hours: OEG lists it as open all day ('hoạt động cả ngày'), but see the tip on the legal 22:00–08:00 limit
  - Address: 318 Lạc Long Quân, Tây Hồ, Hà Nội
  - Price: 14,300 VND/h (Net Hub: RTX 3060, 240 Hz); Premium (RTX 5050, 420 Hz) 18,700; Hyper Space 23,100; private and couple rooms 27,500 (OEG site, Oct 2026)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=OEG%20Cyber%20Hub%2C%20318%20L%E1%BA%A1c%20Long%20Qu%C3%A2n%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://cyber.oeg.vn/318-lac-long-quan)

#### Escape rooms

- **★ Miss Terry Escape Rooms** · Old Quarter (Hoàn Kiếm) · $$ · *checked 2026*
  - Hanoi's best-known escape-room brand runs its games in English as well as Vietnamese, with English-speaking game masters. The Old Quarter branch has two story-heavy rooms: Blood Dragon, a heist in a 1990s Hong Kong triad bar, and Room 1704, a horror room set in a cursed motel.
  - Tip: Book on the English site and arrive 15 minutes early for the briefing. In rooms with an age limit (Room 1704 is 13+), every player must show hard-copy ID with name, photo and date of birth, so bring your passport. Play Monday to Friday before 17:00 to avoid the weekend surcharge. Groups of up to 8 should use the 24 Hoàng Quốc Việt rooms (Secret Agent, Heist of the Century) or the 168 Ngọc Khánh rooms (Down the Rabbit Hole, Secrets of the Jungle). The 168 Ngọc Khánh branch replaced 381 Đội Cấn, but the English homepage still shows the old address, so confirm when you book.
  - Hours: Daily 10:00–22:30; last games start 21:00–21:30
  - Address: 70 Hàng Bạc, Hoàn Kiếm, Hà Nội; also 11th floor, 24 Hoàng Quốc Việt (Cầu Giấy) and 168 Ngọc Khánh (Ba Đình)
  - Price: 500k VND per team of 2, 650k for 3–4, +150k per extra player, plus 8% VAT; +100k per game from Friday 17:00 to Sunday
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Miss%20Terry%20Escape%20Room%2C%2070%20H%C3%A0ng%20B%E1%BA%A1c%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://missterry.vn/en/)

#### Consoles, VR & arcades

- **Multi Lounge at Grand Plaza Hanoi** (Multi Lounge – Khách sạn Grand Plaza Hà Nội) · Cầu Giấy (Trần Duy Hưng boulevard) · *checked 2026*
  - Hanoi's most comfortable console room opened in 2026 inside the five-star Grand Plaza and is open to non-guests. It has PS5 racing rigs with wheel and pedals for Gran Turismo, a Nintendo Switch 2 loaded with Mario Kart and Mario Party, and 10 RTX 3060 PCs set up as two five-seat pods.
  - Tip: It suits a rainy-day group session or a private 5v5 across the two PC pods, and the PS5s also have Rocket League and FIFA/PES. Hours and prices aren't published, so call +84 24 3555 1000 or email info@grandplazahanoi.com first. It is separate from the hotel's electronic-gaming (casino) room.
  - Address: Grand Plaza Hanoi Hotel, 117 Trần Duy Hưng, Yên Hòa ward, Hà Nội
  - Price: Prices not published; ask the hotel
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Grand%20Plaza%20Hanoi%20Hotel%2C%20117%20Tr%E1%BA%A7n%20Duy%20H%C6%B0ng%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://www.grandplazahanoi.com/vi/multi-lounge)
- **Timezone (Vincom Metropolis; bowling at AEON Mall Hà Đông)** · Liễu Giai, Ba Đình (Metropolis); Dương Nội, Hà Đông (bowling branch) · $$ · *checked 2026*
  - The most polished arcade chain in Hanoi has VR rides (Space Ride VR, Warship VR), racing machines and gun cabinets. Its 1,300 m² AEON Mall Hà Đông branch adds Social Bowling lanes that don't need bowling shoes.
  - Tip: Load a Timezone card with Tizo credits, because top-up bonuses run most weeks. Vincom Metropolis is the most central branch, and the newest is Timezone Hanoi Centre (B2, 175 Nguyễn Thái Học). Bowling at Hà Đông is first come, first served, with no booking needed.
  - Hours: Vincom Metropolis: daily 9:30–22:00. AEON Mall Hà Đông: Mon–Fri 10:00–22:00, Sat–Sun 9:00–22:00
  - Address: 3rd floor, Vincom Metropolis, 29 Liễu Giai, Ba Đình; bowling: 3rd floor, AEON Mall Hà Đông, Dương Nội
  - Price: Pay per play from a reloadable card; top-up promotions change often (late July 2026: 1,500 Tizo for a 750k VND top-up)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Timezone%20Vincom%20Metropolis%2C%2029%20Li%E1%BB%85u%20Giai%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://www.timezonegames.com/vi-vn/dia-diem/ha-noi/vincom-metropolis/)

#### Archery & crossbow

- **Archery Fun Club** (Archery Fun – sân bắn cung) · Xuân La – Võ Chí Công road (northwest of West Lake) · $$ · *likely open*
  - You shoot at targets in a hidden garden of tall trees and birdsong, with coaching, bows and protective gear included. It is Hanoi's most foreigner-friendly real-world shooting option, listed on English booking sites, with an optional BBQ and drinks.
  - Tip: Book through GetYourGuide or Viator (about US$16) or message ahead, because the entrance is tucked down a lane. Vietnamese listings say it opens only on Saturdays, Sundays and holidays while the English booking pages say daily, so confirm your slot. Plan 1–3 hours, and add the BBQ for a slow afternoon.
  - Hours: 9:00–19:00; a Nov 2025 Vietnamese listing says Saturdays, Sundays and holidays only, while English booking pages say daily
  - Address: Ngõ 79 Võ Chí Công (K2 Daewoo), Xuân Đỉnh ward, Hà Nội
  - Price: About US$16 per person on booking sites, with coaching and gear; Vietnamese listings quote 90–200k VND per walk-in session (2025); food and drinks extra
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Archery%20Fun%20Club%2C%20ng%C3%B5%2079%20V%C3%B5%20Ch%C3%AD%20C%C3%B4ng%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://theabroadguide.com/play-archery-in-hanoi/)
- **Target Archery** (CLB bắn cung Hà Nội – Target Archery) · Thạch Bàn, Long Biên (east bank, near AEON Mall Long Biên) · $$ · *likely open*
  - It bills itself as Hanoi's first archery club for young people. Shooting distances run from 6 m to 50 m, and instructors coach stance and aim, so it works for first-timers and serious archers alike.
  - Tip: You pay by the hour and the lane fee drops after the first hour, so a 2–3 hour session is good value. Pair it with bowling and the arcades at AEON Mall Long Biên nearby.
  - Hours: 9:00–19:00
  - Address: Số 3, ngõ 184 Thạch Bàn, Long Biên, Hà Nội
  - Price: Lane fee 150k VND for the first hour, 100k for the second and 50k for the third, plus bow rental 100–300k; listings quote 150–300k per session (2025)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Target%20Archery%2C%20ng%C3%B5%20184%20Th%E1%BA%A1ch%20B%C3%A0n%2C%20Long%20Bi%C3%AAn%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://baolamdong.vn/ban-cung-ha-noi-6-san-tap-ly-tuong-de-giai-toa-cang-thang-401433.html)
- **Cổ Loa Citadel crossbow shooting** (Thành Cổ Loa – trải nghiệm bắn nỏ; Lễ hội đền Cổ Loa) · Cổ Loa, Đông Anh (about 17 km north of central Hanoi) · *likely open*
  - This is the one place where firing a crossbow (nỏ) means something. Legend says King An Dương Vương's magic crossbow defended Âu Lạc from this citadel more than 2,000 years ago, and the site now runs crossbow sessions for visitors, while crossbow shooting is the highlight game at the annual temple festival.
  - Tip: Outside festival time no crossbow schedule is published, so ask the relic site before making the 17 km Grab trip. For the festival, go on the main day, 11 Feb 2027 (the 6th day of Tết), when the morning procession runs. Then queue for the crossbow game, which runs alongside human chess and wrestling.
  - Hours: Festival: 6th–18th day of the first lunar month (11–23 Feb 2027, main day 11 Feb). The year-round crossbow sessions have no published schedule.
  - Address: Cổ Loa Citadel relic site, Cổ Loa, Đông Anh, Hà Nội
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Th%C3%A0nh%20C%E1%BB%95%20Loa%2C%20C%E1%BB%95%20Loa%2C%20%C4%90%C3%B4ng%20Anh%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://hanoionline.vn/hao-khi-xua-thuc-day-tai-thanh-co-loa-389053.htm)

#### Bowling & board games

- **Dream Games Bowling (AEON Mall Long Biên)** (Dream Games Bowling) · Long Biên (east bank of the Red River) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - Hanoi's best late-night bowling: 16 lanes on the mall's 4th floor, which AEON calls one of the biggest lane counts in Vietnam, with a billiards zone alongside. It stays open until midnight at weekends.
  - Tip: Games are cheapest 10:00–13:00 and after 22:00 on weekdays. On member days (the 5th and 20th of each month), you get a third game free when you buy two. The mall's 3rd floor has the Timezone and Dream Games arcades, and Target Archery is a short Grab ride away. Phone 024 3200 3190.
  - Hours: Mon–Fri 10:00–23:00; Sat, Sun and holidays 9:00–24:00
  - Address: 4th floor, AEON Mall Long Biên, 27 Cổ Linh, Long Biên, Hà Nội
  - Price: 35–60k VND per person per game depending on time and day; 250–300k per lane-hour; shoes 10k, socks 12k (2024 price list)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Dream%20Games%20Bowling%2C%20AEON%20Mall%20Long%20Bi%C3%AAn%2C%2027%20C%E1%BB%95%20Linh%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://aeonmall-long-bien.com.vn/cua-hang/dream-bowling/)
- **The Root Board Game Café** (The Root Boardgame Coffee) · Hai Bà Trưng (Trần Xuân Soạn, near Hôm market) · $ · *likely open*
  - A roomy board-game café near Hôm market with private rooms, staff who teach rules, and shelves that keep adding rare titles. It also stocks Thần Tích, a Vietnamese-made card battler whose characters come from Vietnamese myths and fairy tales.
  - Tip: Ask staff to teach you Thần Tích. Many boxes are Vietnamese editions, so pick language-light games such as Splendor, Dixit or Exploding Kittens. Weekday afternoons are quieter than evenings, when students pile in. To reserve a room, call 0325 762 750 or message its Facebook page (The Root Board Game Cafe). For tournament nights, try Minipolo in Đống Đa instead.
  - Hours: 9:00–23:00
  - Address: 72 Trần Xuân Soạn, Hai Bà Trưng, Hà Nội
  - Price: About 30–60k VND per person, charged as drinks
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=The%20Root%20Boardgame%20Coffee%2C%2072%20Tr%E1%BA%A7n%20Xu%C3%A2n%20So%E1%BA%A1n%2C%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i) · [Source](https://boardgame.vn/blogs/tin-tuc/board-game-ha-noi-10-dia-diem-choi-hap-dan-cua-hang-chat-luong-nhat)

### Hanoi: Books & literary (Sách & văn chương)

Literary Hanoi runs on two tracks: a thousand years of Confucian scholarship at the Temple of Literature, where royal doctoral laureates are carved into stone steles, and a lively Vietnamese-language book culture on the 19/12 Book Street, Đinh Lễ and in the city's book cafés. English readers have two proper bookshops, The Bookworm and The Travellers' Book Shop; everywhere else, come for the atmosphere, the handsome Vietnamese editions and the scholarly rituals that peak at Tết, when calligraphers brush new-year characters beside Văn Lake.

#### Bookshops & book streets

- **★ Hanoi Book Street (19/12 Book Street)** (Phố Sách Hà Nội (phố 19 Tháng 12)) · French Quarter (Cửa Nam ward), next to Hỏa Lò Prison · $ · *checked 2026*
  - A shaded, car-free 200 m street that has been Hanoi's book showcase since 2017, lined with stalls run by publishers such as Kim Đồng and Nhã Nam, with benches and small cafés in between. It is the city's stage for book festivals: in April 2026 it hosted 14 publishers and distributors at 15 stalls for Vietnam Book and Reading Culture Day.
  - Tip: Most stock is in Vietnamese, so come for the atmosphere and the handsome Vietnamese editions; Nhã Nam's illustrated Hanoi books make good souvenirs, such as the artbook edition of Thạch Lam's 1943 'Hà Nội băm sáu phố phường' (236,000 VND list price). Weekday mornings are quiet, weekends bring signings and children's workshops, and the biggest festival comes at Tết. Pair it with Hỏa Lò Prison next door, then walk about 800 m to Đinh Lễ.
  - Hours: Daily about 8:00–22:00 (individual stalls vary)
  - Address: Phố 19 Tháng 12, between Lý Thường Kiệt and Hai Bà Trưng, Cửa Nam ward
  - Price: Free to browse; festival weeks bring discounts on new titles (30–50% off in April 2026)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Ph%E1%BB%91%20S%C3%A1ch%20H%C3%A0%20N%E1%BB%99i%2C%20ph%E1%BB%91%2019%20Th%C3%A1ng%2012%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://nhandan.vn/chum-su-kien-tai-pho-sach-ha-noi-nhan-ngay-sach-va-van-hoa-doc-viet-nam-2026-post956829.html)
- **★ The Bookworm Hanoi** · Trúc Bạch (Ba Đình) · $$ · *checked 2026*
  - Hanoi's English-language bookshop since 2001, with more than 20,000 new and second-hand titles in almost every genre across two colourful floors. A dedicated room holds Vietnamese literature in translation, photo books and cookbooks, and there is a café in the adjoining courtyard.
  - Tip: Old map pins and guides still send people to 44 Châu Long; go to 44 Phạm Hồng Thái. Bring finished paperbacks, because the shop buys used books for resale, and ask the staff which Vietnamese novels to read in translation before settling into the courtyard café.
  - Hours: Daily 9:00–19:00
  - Address: 44 Phạm Hồng Thái, Ba Đình (moved from 44 Châu Long)
  - Price: English translations of Vietnamese fiction about 290–380k VND; new imports about 350–480k; used copies cheaper
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=The%20Bookworm%2C%2044%20Ph%E1%BA%A1m%20H%E1%BB%93ng%20Th%C3%A1i%2C%20Ba%20%C4%90%C3%ACnh%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://www.bookwormhanoi.com/about-bookworm)
- **The Travellers' Book Shop** · Old Quarter (Hoàn Kiếm) · *checked 2026*
  - A small, independent Old Quarter shop for English and French books, new, used and remaindered, with dedicated shelves of Vietnamese authors and books on Vietnam: Bảo Ninh's The Sorrow of War, Vũ Trọng Phụng's 1930s Hanoi satire Dumb Luck, Nguyễn Du's Tale of Kiều.
  - Tip: The most central place to pick up a Vietnam novel or a street-food cookbook. To check a title first, WhatsApp Ms Thu on +84 93 663 3358 (the number on the shop's website). Sources disagree on the street number, so look along Hàng Giầy around Nos. 27–31.
  - Hours: Mon–Sat 8:00–19:00, Sun 8:00–17:00 (per its website)
  - Address: 31 Hàng Giầy (Znews, Sept 2026); the shop's website lists 27 Hàng Giầy
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=The%20Travellers%27%20Book%20Shop%2C%20H%C3%A0ng%20Gi%E1%BA%A7y%2C%20Ho%C3%A0n%20Ki%E1%BA%BFm%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://travellersbookshop.com/)
- **Đinh Lễ Book Street & Mão Bookshop** (Phố sách Đinh Lễ & Nhà sách Mão) · Hoàn Kiếm Lake, behind the Hanoi Post Office (Hoàn Kiếm) · $ · *checked 2025*
  - Hanoi's original book street grew from the pavement stall run by Phạm Thị Mão and her husband, retired teacher Lê Luy, by the lakeside post office. Their bookshop, up a stairwell in the old apartment block at No. 5, is a roughly 200 m² warren of new, out-of-print and rare Vietnamese books, with a spiral staircase and tables where you can read all day for free.
  - Tip: Treat it as a 30-minute detour: the street is quieter than in its heyday, with cafés and stationery shops among the bookshops. Climb up to Mão, dig through the discount piles, then walk about 800 m to Hanoi Book Street. There are few English titles.
  - Hours: Street shops roughly 8:00–22:00; Mão about 9:00 to 17:00–18:00 on weekdays, later at weekends (listings, unconfirmed)
  - Address: Đinh Lễ street, off Tràng Tiền; Mão is on the 2nd floor of the apartment block in alley No. 5 Đinh Lễ
  - Price: Free to browse and read; discount piles of older titles
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Nh%C3%A0%20s%C3%A1ch%20M%C3%A3o%2C%205%20%C4%90inh%20L%E1%BB%85%2C%20Ho%C3%A0n%20Ki%E1%BA%BFm%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://mia.vn/cam-nang-du-lich/kham-pha-nha-sach-mao-ha-noi-17750)

#### Literary landmarks

- **★ Temple of Literature (Văn Miếu – Quốc Tử Giám)** (Văn Miếu – Quốc Tử Giám) · Văn Miếu – Quốc Tử Giám ward (old Đống Đa), about 2.5 km from the Old Quarter · $ · *checked 2026*
  - Vietnam's literary shrine: a temple to Confucius founded in 1070 and the country's first university (1076), where 82 stone steles on tortoises, erected from 1484, carve the names of royal doctoral laureates and are on UNESCO's Memory of the World register. 2026 is the academy's 950th year, marked this autumn with exhibitions and a national ceremony.
  - Tip: Arrive at 8:00 to have the steles to yourself, or book the 'Tinh hoa đạo học' night tour: 3D mapping on the story of the steles, a meeting with a thầy đồ scholar and a woodblock print of the Khuê Văn Các pavilion to take home (call 033 644 1838). Inside, look for calligraphy try-outs and the Thư quán reading café. Expect restoration scaffolding (Khuê Văn Các is due to finish in Nov 2026) and security around the 950th-anniversary ceremony on the evening of Sat 14 Nov 2026, when the night tour may not run. Don't touch the steles, and dress modestly in the worship halls.
  - Hours: Daily 8:00–17:00; night tour Wed, Sat & Sun 18:30–22:00
  - Address: Main entrance: 58 Quốc Tử Giám
  - Price: Day ticket 70,000 VND adults, 35,000 concessions, free under 16. Night tour 299,000 VND adult / 149,000 child for one show, 399,000 / 199,000 for two shows
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=V%C4%83n%20Mi%E1%BA%BFu%20%E2%80%93%20Qu%E1%BB%91c%20T%E1%BB%AD%20Gi%C3%A1m%2C%2058%20Qu%E1%BB%91c%20T%E1%BB%AD%20Gi%C3%A1m%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://vanmieu.gov.vn/vi/introduction/visitor-information)
- **Museum of Vietnamese Literature** (Bảo tàng Văn học Việt Nam) · West Lake (Tây Hồ), off Âu Cơ road · $ · *checked 2026*
  - The Vietnam Writers' Association's own museum, on the site of its old Quảng Bá writing school, traces Vietnamese literature from the Lý dynasty to today across three floors. Look for Nguyễn Du's wooden desk, a recreated village schoolroom, manuscripts such as Tô Hoài's handwritten 'Chuyện nhà Chử', and the statues of classical authors in the grounds.
  - Tip: It opens on weekdays only, so phone the hotline 086 505 1179 before you go, and ask at reception for a guided explanation (thuyết minh). Pair it with West Lake cafés.
  - Hours: Mon–Fri 8:00–12:00 and 13:30–17:00; closed Saturdays, Sundays and public holidays
  - Address: Ngõ 275 Âu Cơ, Tây Hồ
  - Price: Adults 40,000 VND; high-school and university students 30,000; primary and lower-secondary pupils 20,000
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=B%E1%BA%A3o%20t%C3%A0ng%20V%C4%83n%20h%E1%BB%8Dc%20Vi%E1%BB%87t%20Nam%2C%20Ng%C3%B5%20275%20%C3%82u%20C%C6%A1%2C%20T%C3%A2y%20H%E1%BB%93%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://baotangvanhoc.vn/gio-mo-cua/)
- **Vũ Dân Tân Museum & Vũ Đình Long Room** (Bảo tàng Vũ Dân Tân) · Ngọc Thụy, Long Biên (across the Red River) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - Opened in December 2025 on the studio site of contemporary-art pioneer Vũ Dân Tân, the museum also holds the archive of his father Vũ Đình Long, whose 1921 play 'Chén thuốc độc' launched modern Vietnamese spoken theatre and who ran the Tân Dân publishing house. Until 25 Nov 2026 the open studio 'The Writers' Unmasking' hangs Tân's unnamed 2009 portraits of Tân Dân's authors and asks you to guess who is who.
  - Tip: The open studio is on the 3rd floor; buy tickets at the counter (entry is free only on an exhibition's opening day). Watch Hanoi Grapevine for the museum's free, register-ahead reading circles, supported by Goethe-Institut and limited to about 20 places (readings in Vietnamese). It's across the river, so take a Grab.
  - Hours: 10:00–17:00 per museum listings (check closing days on its Facebook); 'The Writers' Unmasking' runs 3 Oct–25 Nov 2026
  - Address: 443 Ngọc Thụy, Long Biên
  - Price: Adults 40,000 VND; students 20,000; free under 18 and over 60; talks free with registration
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=B%E1%BA%A3o%20t%C3%A0ng%20V%C5%A9%20D%C3%A2n%20T%C3%A2n%2C%20443%20Ng%E1%BB%8Dc%20Th%E1%BB%A5y%2C%20Long%20Bi%C3%AAn%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://hanoigrapevine.com/2026/09/open-studio-the-writers-unmasking-a-game-of-literary-memory/)
- **Thạch Lam's 'Hà Nội băm sáu phố phường' walk** (Hà Nội băm sáu phố phường (Thạch Lam, 1943)) · Old Quarter to Hoàn Kiếm Lake (Hoàn Kiếm) · $ · *likely open*
  - Thạch Lam (1910–1942) of the Tự Lực literary group wrote these essays about loving Hanoi 'with the soul of a Hanoian', published in 1943. Walk the places he said still held old Hanoi, from the Ô Quan Chưởng gate through the alleys Ngõ Phất Lộc and Ngõ Trung Yên to Hàng Đường, where he admired the calligraphy of old shop signs, and finish at Ngọc Sơn Temple on Hoàn Kiếm Lake.
  - Tip: Read the essays free on Vietnamese Wikisource first. At Ngọc Sơn, which honours Văn Xương, patron of scholars, find the Pen Tower inscribed 'Tả thanh thiên' ('write on the blue sky') and the Ink-Slab gate, added when the scholar Nguyễn Văn Siêu restored the temple in 1865. In autumn buy green young-rice cốm and eat it slowly, as his cốm essay insists; for a detour, go to Yên Phụ on West Lake, where his house drew writers such as Thế Lữ and Nguyễn Tuân.
  - Hours: Any time; daylight is best. Ngọc Sơn Temple: about 7:00–18:00 on weekdays, later at weekends (travel listings)
  - Address: Ô Quan Chưởng gate → Ngõ Phất Lộc → Ngõ Trung Yên → Hàng Đường → Hoàn Kiếm Lake & Ngọc Sơn Temple
  - Price: The walk is free; Ngọc Sơn Temple ticket about 30,000 VND per travel listings (not officially confirmed)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=%C3%94%20Quan%20Ch%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Fng%2C%20H%C3%A0ng%20Chi%E1%BA%BFu%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://vi.wikisource.org/wiki/Hà_Nội_băm_sáu_phố_phường)
- **Graham Greene & Somerset Maugham at the Metropole** (Khách sạn Sofitel Legend Metropole Hà Nội) · French Quarter (Hoàn Kiếm) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - Hanoi's grand hotel, opened in 1901, is its most literary address: Somerset Maugham stayed in 1923 and Graham Greene in 1951, and both have suites named after them. For the 125th anniversary in 2026, the Heritage and Opera wing lobbies hold a photo-history exhibition running from colonial Indochina to the 1990s.
  - Tip: Non-guests can walk the lobby exhibition and pair it with a drink at Bamboo Bar, already in this guide; the Metropole History Book is sold at La Boutique. The historian-led 'Path of History' tour of the wartime bomb shelter is for hotel guests (daily 17:00 and 18:00, via the concierge), and Greene pilgrims can book the Graham Greene Suite. Reread 'The Quiet American' before Ninh Bình: Fowler goes to Phát Diệm to witness a battle.
  - Hours: Lobby exhibition during hotel hours; Path of History tours daily at 17:00 & 18:00 (hotel guests only)
  - Address: 15 Ngô Quyền
  - Price: Lobby exhibition free to see; drinks, the suites and the guests-only tour are splurges
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Sofitel%20Legend%20Metropole%20Hanoi%2C%2015%20Ng%C3%B4%20Quy%E1%BB%81n%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://www.sofitel-legend-metropole-hanoi.com/accommodation/graham-greene-suite/)

#### Literary events

- **★ Tết Calligraphy Fair at Văn Lake** (Hội chữ Xuân – Không gian Hồ Văn, Văn Miếu – Quốc Tử Giám) · Văn Miếu – Quốc Tử Giám ward (old Đống Đa) · *likely open*
  - Hanoi's great Tết ritual of 'xin chữ', asking a scholar to brush an auspicious character for the new year, held every Lunar New Year beside Văn Lake. In 2026 it filled 35 tents with selected calligraphers, alongside heritage exhibitions, craft demonstrations, folk games and traditional music.
  - Tip: 2027 dates usually come out in January. Tết Đinh Mùi is Sat 6 Feb 2027, and on the 2026 pattern (24th day of the last lunar month to the 13th of the first) expect roughly 30/31 Jan to 18 Feb 2027. Choose your word before you queue (Tâm 'heart', Nhẫn 'patience', Học 'learning'), agree the price before the brush touches the paper, let the ink dry before rolling it up, and go on a weekday morning for shorter queues.
  - Music: The 2026 programme included quan họ, ca trù and chèo performances
  - Hours: 2026 edition: 11 Feb–1 Mar, daily 8:00–22:00. 2027: not yet announced (expected late Jan to mid-Feb 2027)
  - Address: Hồ Văn (Văn Lake), across Quốc Tử Giám street from the Temple of Literature gate
  - Price: Fair admission and calligraphy fees not published; the 70,000 VND Văn Miếu ticket applies inside the temple
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=H%E1%BB%93%20V%C4%83n%2C%20Qu%E1%BB%91c%20T%E1%BB%AD%20Gi%C3%A1m%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://baovanhoa.vn/van-hoa/tung-bung-khai-hoi-chu-xuan-binh-ngo-2026-204263.html)
- **Hanoi Poetry Day at Văn Miếu** (Ngày thơ Hà Nội (hưởng ứng Ngày thơ Việt Nam, Rằm tháng Giêng)) · Văn Miếu – Quốc Tử Giám ward (old Đống Đa) · *likely open*
  - The national Vietnam Poetry Day no longer takes place at Văn Miếu, but the Hanoi Writers' Association's own annual Poetry Day still fills the temple with poets just before the Lantern Festival. The 2026 edition (1 Mar, theme 'Vươn mình ra biển lớn') mixed readings with poems set to music and adapted for chèo, recitation contests and poetry-club displays in the Thái Học courtyard.
  - Tip: The 2027 date isn't announced. The Lantern Festival falls on Sat 20 Feb 2027 and the Hanoi event opened one to two days before it in 2024 and 2026, so expect about 18–20 Feb 2027 and watch vanvn.vn in January. The programme is in Vietnamese; go for the spectacle of Văn Miếu full of poets.
  - Music: Poems set to music, chèo adaptations and traditional sung recitation (2026 programme)
  - Hours: 2027 date not announced (expected about 18–20 Feb 2027); the 2026 edition opened on the morning of Sun 1 Mar
  - Address: Văn Miếu – Quốc Tử Giám, 58 Quốc Tử Giám
  - Price: Admission terms not published; Văn Miếu's 70,000 VND ticket may apply
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=V%C4%83n%20Mi%E1%BA%BFu%20%E2%80%93%20Qu%E1%BB%91c%20T%E1%BB%AD%20Gi%C3%A1m%2C%2058%20Qu%E1%BB%91c%20T%E1%BB%AD%20Gi%C3%A1m%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://vanvn.vn/ngay-tho-ha-noi-2026-gui-gam-khat-vong-vuon-minh-ra-bien-lon/)

#### Book cafés & libraries

- **Tranquil Books & Coffee** · Ba Đình (villa branch); western edge of the Old Quarter (original) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - Hanoi's best-known book café, open since 2015, with wooden floors, raw brick and shelves to the ceiling holding literature, art and some foreign-language titles. The Cao Bá Quát branch, on a street named after the 19th-century poet, sits in a French villa with seats among the trees.
  - Tip: Choose the Cao Bá Quát villa for a long reading afternoon and the original Nguyễn Quang Bích lane café for a quick Old Quarter break. Regulars order the cold brew or a matcha latte; check its social pages for evening events.
  - Music: A January 2025 write-up mentioned Tuesday-evening open mic nights (unconfirmed for 2026)
  - Hours: About 8:00–22:30 daily (Cao Bá Quát; other branches vary)
  - Address: Villa branch: 19 Cao Bá Quát, Ba Đình; original (2015): 5 Nguyễn Quang Bích, Hoàn Kiếm; other branches at 8 Nguyễn Quang Bích, 15B Trần Hưng Đạo and 18B Nguyễn Biểu
  - Price: Drinks about 40–80k VND (Aug 2026)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Tranquil%20Books%20%26%20Coffee%2C%2019%20Cao%20B%C3%A1%20Qu%C3%A1t%2C%20Ba%20%C4%90%C3%ACnh%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://znews.vn/5-quan-ca-phe-sach-tron-dong-dip-29-post1679481.html)
- **Tender (bookshop-bar)** (Tender Bar) · Ngọc Khánh area (Ba Đình), off Nguyễn Chí Thanh · *checked 2026*
  - Opened in 2021 as a consignment shelf for books, Tender is now a deep-red bookshop crammed to the ceiling among cassette players and antiques, and a stockist for independent publishers. By evening the quiet café-bookshop turns into a spot for cocktails, books and music.
  - Tip: Arrive late afternoon to browse before the bar fills up, and ask for the handwritten list of house specials kept at the bar; the classic cocktails are well made too. It's hidden down a lane, so take a Grab, and check opening days first (it closed on Tuesdays in 2023).
  - Music: Music-led evenings; Saigoneer (2023) reported live music and movie nights in the rooftop garden
  - Hours: Reported in 2023 as about 10:00 to near midnight, closed Tuesdays; check before going
  - Address: 8 Ngõ 29 Nguyễn Chí Thanh
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Tender%20Bar%2C%208%20Ng%C3%B5%2029%20Nguy%E1%BB%85n%20Ch%C3%AD%20Thanh%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://znews.vn/di-bar-doc-sach-o-ha-noi-post1683462.html)
- **National Library of Vietnam** (Thư viện Quốc gia Việt Nam) · Tràng Thi, west of Hoàn Kiếm Lake (Cửa Nam ward) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - Founded in 1917 as the Central Library of Indochina and opened to readers in 1919, the national library holds about 2.5 million items, including some 5,280 Hán Nôm manuscripts and 68,500 Indochina-era documents. It puts on free themed document exhibitions a few minutes' walk from Hanoi Book Street.
  - Tip: To sit in the reading rooms, foreigners staying in Vietnam can get a reader card with a passport: 120,000 VND for 12 months, issued 5–10 minutes after you fill in the form (card desk Mon–Sat 8:00–11:30 and 13:30–16:30; you can also apply online). It closes on national holidays, over Tết and on one internal day a month (8 Oct 2026).
  - Hours: Daily 8:00–20:00; closed national holidays, Tết and one internal day a month
  - Address: 31 Tràng Thi, Cửa Nam ward
  - Price: Exhibitions free; reader card 120,000 VND for 12 months
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Th%C6%B0%20vi%E1%BB%87n%20Qu%E1%BB%91c%20gia%20Vi%E1%BB%87t%20Nam%2C%2031%20Tr%C3%A0ng%20Thi%2C%20Hanoi) · [Source](https://nlv.gov.vn/tro-giup-ban-doc/thu-tuc-lam-the.html)

### Hanoi: local tips

- How bia hơi works: it is a light (about 4%) unpasteurised lager brewed and delivered daily, so it is drunk the same day. The peak is the 4–7pm after-work rush, and most bia hơi places close by 10–11pm. Staff keep count and you pay when you leave, usually in cash. Toast with 'Một, hai, ba, dzô!' and order lạc rang, đậu rán or nem chua rán.
- Happy hours make the big names affordable. The Hudson Rooms: 2-for-1 5:30–7:30pm. angelina at the Metropole: free second drink 4–7pm. Polite & Co: 5–8pm. Terraço: 30% off 5–7pm. Sunset Bar: 2-for-1 'Làng' cocktails 6–8pm. Several deals are per person, not per table.
- Read the small print. Hotel prices marked '++' add about 15% (5% service plus 10% VAT on the Summit and Metropole menus), while USEbar and The Haflington include VAT and service. Expect about 200–450k VND for a serious cocktail.
- Sunset falls around 5:15–6pm from October to February and about 6:40pm in June–July. Be seated 30–45 minutes early at The Summit, Terraço or Sunset Bar, and book weekend sunset tables. Haze can mute the views, so make the most of clear days.
- Book Workshop14, and The Haflington on weekend nights. Most small bars take bookings by Instagram or Facebook message. Since the 2025 ward reform, give Grab drivers the street address (e.g. '94 Hàng Mã', '6 Ngõ 5 Từ Hoa') rather than an old district name.
- Closing time is elastic. Old Quarter police walk through around 11:45pm, and bars pull their shutters halfway down and carry on; Tây Hồ venues run later. Don't ride a motorbike after drinking: under Decree 168/2024 any alcohol means a 2–3 million VND fine, rising to 8–10 million VND and a 22–24-month licence suspension above 80 mg/100 ml. Use Grab, Xanh SM or a taxi.
- More to drink. 'Vang vỉa' (pavement wine) is the 2025–26 trend: Bouchon (69 Phùng Hưng or 87 Quán Thánh; 2-for-1 wine 3–6pm) and Cork & Bottle (50 Quảng An by West Lake; 2-for-1 wine 4–7pm). For craft beer, try Pasteur Street's taproom behind St Joseph's Cathedral (1 Ấu Triệu; weekly live music) or 7 Bridges' lakeside terrace (104 Xuân Diệu).
- For a cheap Trúc Bạch Lake crawl, pair Standing Bar with street-side classic cocktails at DOT (66 Trấn Vũ) and Lang Thang (8B Lạc Chính) around the corner.
- Order like a local: milk coffee is 'nâu' (iced: 'nâu đá'), black is 'đen' or 'đen đá', and 'bạc xỉu' is the milkiest. Egg coffee is 'cà phê trứng', yogurt coffee 'sữa chua cà phê' and coconut coffee 'cốt dừa cà phê'; add 'ít ngọt' for less sweet. Hot egg coffee arrives standing in a bowl of hot water, so leave it there and eat the foam with the spoon.
- 2026 prices: at street cafés, đen đá costs 20–35k VND and nâu 25–40k. Egg coffee is about 35–60k at the heritage cafés, drinks at design and view cafés run 50–90k, and a specialty pour-over is about 70–150k. Carry cash for the old family cafés; tipping isn't expected.
- Hidden cafés sit behind shops, down corridors and up dim stairwells, so look for small signs at alley mouths. 'Tầng 2' means the 2nd floor, 'ngõ' a lane and 'tập thể' a collective housing block, and alley signs ask you to push your bike with the engine off. Since the mid-2025 ward merger, official addresses use new ward names, so search Maps by café name or by house number plus street.
- Timing: for lake, balcony and rooftop seats, go before 9am or in the hour before sunset; weekend evenings are the busiest. To skip the queue at Giảng, go between 9:30 and 11:00 on a weekday. Most specialty bars open around 8am, but Kafeville and Ta Cà Phê close at 6pm, while RAAW Hàng Buồm stays open until 11pm.
- Easy crawls: Nguyễn Hữu Huân has Giảng (No. 39) and Lâm (No. 60). Hàng Hành lane by the lake is lined with pavement cafés, including Nhân (39D), a 1946 institution. VULAB (30B) and ACID8 (34) are a few doors apart on Xóm Hà Hồi, off Trần Hưng Đạo. Around the Cathedral, refined., Loading T, Blackbird and Miên Man are all within a five-minute walk.
- Taste Vietnam side by side: try a Fine Robusta through a phin (refined., 30S Coffea) against a Sơn La or Đà Lạt arabica on V60 (RAAW, Ta Cà Phê), plus liberica (cà phê mít) at RAAW. Bags of Vietnamese specialty beans (about 60–220k VND) make light souvenirs. Skip souvenir-shop 'weasel coffee', an overpriced novelty that raises animal-welfare concerns.
- Hanoi's café scene churns, and older lists still send people to places that are gone: Cư Xá (closed 31 Aug 2026), Trill Rooftop (closed Oct 2025), Hip Hub (closed) and the Highlands in the Shark Jaw building (demolished 2025). Check recent Google Maps reviews or Facebook posts before crossing town.
- Train Street (Oct 2026): the trackside cafés are illegal but tolerated. Tour companies have been barred since March 2025, police clear the tracks periodically, and there have been fewer trains since late February 2026. If you go anyway, buy a drink, sit against the wall, never stand on the rails, and ignore 'train in 5 minutes' hustles; ask to see the timetable.
- Plan jazz by the day of the week: Binh Minh plays every night 21:00–23:15, Blake's Thu–Sun, and Long Waits' house band Fri–Sat (Thursday is its open stage). Monday is the weakest night. The rooms are spread around Hoàn Kiếm, so pick one per night and budget a cover of about 100–250k VND on top of drinks.
- A perfect Sunday: free jazz at the Octagon House (Nhà Bát Giác) from 15:30, then a loop of the lake's walking street (Fri–Sun, 18:00–24:00 from October to March), then Binh Minh's 21:00 set behind the Opera House.
- Club nights are announced late on Instagram and Resident Advisor; buy on RA (Unmute is about 226k VND online versus 300–400k at the door). Police raid clubs and drug-test on the spot, nitrous balloons have been banned since mid-2025, and foreigners who test positive face deportation or prison, so stay clean, carry ID and leave quietly if a night is shut down.
- Traditional music at weekends: the Old Quarter relics Bạch Mã Temple (76 Hàng Buồm), Kim Ngân communal house (42–44 Hàng Bạc) and Quan Đế Temple (28 Hàng Buồm) open Fri–Sun 19:00–21:00 (entry 20k VND) and host xẩm, ca trù and chầu văn, but no schedule is published, so ask at the gate earlier in the day. Free xẩm outside the Đồng Xuân Market gate during the Saturday night market is a bonus when it happens. On 23 Nov (Vietnam Cultural Heritage Day), watch for free Old Quarter heritage concerts.
- Big dates from October 2026 to March 2027: BIGBANG at Mỹ Đình Stadium (24–25 Oct) is sold out, so use official resale only; Outpost's Halloween all-nighter is 31 Oct; Lune Production's bamboo-circus 'Làng Tôi – My Village', scored live on folk instruments, starts a Hanoi run on 3 Nov 2026 (luneproduction.com, +84 845 181 188; 800k–1.75M VND); and Hồ Gươm Opera's New Year and post-Tết concerts are TBA. Tết falls on 6 Feb 2027, when many bars and shops close for several days.
- Classical on a budget: the Vietnam National Symphony Orchestra plays its 2026 subscription season at the Academy of Music's Grand Concert Hall (77 Hào Nam), including Rachmaninov's Piano Concerto No. 3 in October 2026 (vnso.org.vn, +84 24 3577 0666). Hanoi Grapevine's listings carry free embassy concerts and SiaM, a donation-based series pairing classical music with ca trù and chèo.
- Phòng trà etiquette: they are listening rooms, so keep chat low during songs and ask about a surcharge (phụ thu) before ordering. Star 'minishow' nights, such as those at Trixie (165 Thái Hà), are priced separately and announced on Facebook.
- Buy tickets direct. The old thanglongwaterpuppet.org and catruthanglong.com domains now carry spam and casino content. Use box offices and official sites: hoguomopera.com, nhahatcheohanoi.vn, vnso.org.vn and luneproduction.com.
- English books: The Bookworm (44 Phạm Hồng Thái, daily 9:00–19:00) is the biggest and buys used books; The Travellers' Book Shop on Hàng Giầy is the most central and also sells French titles. Almost everything else here is Vietnamese-language, so come for the atmosphere and design. Staying a while? Gác Xép Bookstore (gacxepbookstore.vn) sells used English titles online and delivers within Hanoi in 1–2 days; message it before visiting the small storefront.
- Reading list you can buy in English here: Bảo Ninh, The Sorrow of War; Vũ Trọng Phụng, Dumb Luck; Nguyễn Du, The Tale of Kiều; Nguyễn Huy Thiệp's stories; Nguyễn Phan Quế Mai, The Mountains Sing. Thạch Lam's 1943 Hanoi essays are free in Vietnamese on Wikisource.
- French Quarter book loop of about 2 km: Đinh Lễ (climb to Mão at No. 5) → Tràng Tiền → Hạ Hồi lane (The Wiselands library-café, 7:00–23:00) → Hanoi Book Street on 19/12 → National Library at 31 Tràng Thi. Thanh Ninh Books, a new indie bookshop-café at 88 Nguyễn Du (8:30–18:30), is a short walk south. Finish with happy hour (16:00–18:00) at the Library Lounge, La Siesta Classic, 21 Hàng Thùng.
- Văn Miếu prices have gone up: 70,000 VND by day and 299,000–399,000 for the night tour, though many guides still quote 30,000 and 199,000. A national ceremony for the academy's 950th year is planned for 19:30 on Sat 14 Nov 2026, so expect security and possible closures around it; restoration also means scaffolding on Khuê Văn Các (due to finish Nov 2026), the main gate and the Đại Thành hall.
- Tết 2027 (Đinh Mùi) is Sat 6 Feb and the Lantern Festival Sat 20 Feb. Expect the Văn Lake calligraphy fair from about 30/31 Jan to 18 Feb, Hanoi Poetry Day around 18–20 Feb, a Tết festival on Hanoi Book Street and, if repeated, the Hạ Hồi spring book fair (the 2025 edition ran 18 Jan–12 Feb). Dates come out in January, and many small bookshops and cafés close for several days around Tết.
- Readings and talks: check Hanoi Grapevine's Literature and Talk listings, the Institut français (ifv.vn) for touring authors (Đoan Bùi launches 'Xứ sở Vô danh' at Kim Đồng, 55 Quang Trung, on Sun 11 Oct 2026 at 15:00; free, register ahead) and the Vũ Dân Tân Museum's reading circles. Photo-book fans: Matca's 'Read with Us #16' showcase runs to 31 Dec 2026 in Sóng Sánh Café, 48 Ngọc Hà (Tue–Sun 7:00–19:00).
- Literary Hanoi is spread out: the Museum of Vietnamese Literature (Âu Cơ, West Lake) opens on weekdays only, and the Vũ Dân Tân Museum is across the Red River in Ngọc Thụy. Combine one with West Lake cafés, reach the other by Grab, and keep Văn Miếu, the Thạch Lam walk and the Metropole for central days. Greene's The Quiet American sends Fowler to Phát Diệm, so pair the Metropole with that stop on the Ninh Bình leg.
- Useful words: hiệu sách / nhà sách (bookshop), sách cũ (used books), sách ngoại văn (foreign-language books), cà phê sách (book café), thư viện (library), thẻ bạn đọc (reader card), xin chữ (asking a calligrapher for a new-year character).
- Gaming hours: Vietnamese rules bar internet and gaming venues from operating between 22:00 and 08:00. A draft amendment due around November 2026 would let players aged 16+ stay until midnight but turn away under-16s after 21:00 (VnExpress, 4 Sep 2026). Some chains say they are 'open all day', but don't plan on all-night sessions.
- Signing up as a foreigner: at Vikings, staff create a Vikings ID at the cashier, or you can log in at id.vikings.vn with email, Facebook or Discord, and new accounts get 10 free minutes. Topping up in the app needs a Vietnamese phone number and a face photo, so pay at the counter. Each Vikings branch keeps its own balance. At OEG, ask the counter to open a member account.
- Bring your own logins: CS2 and PUBG only need your Steam account, so keep Steam Guard on your phone. Riot accounts stay tied to their home region, so a European or American Valorant or LoL account will connect to faraway servers.
- Guns and the law: never buy, carry or test an air gun, BB gun or replica, because police prosecute air-gun owners as holders of military weapons (Dân trí, 16 May 2026). We found no 2025–26 sign that the paintball fields in Vietnamese listicles operate openly and legally. For live fire, the nearest option is the military-run range at the Củ Chi Tunnels near Saigon.
- Laser tag: Laser Pro at AEON Mall Long Biên has closed. Planet Laser Game (3 Mễ Trì Hạ; 190k VND a round on weekdays and 260k at weekends in 2025 listings) still appears in Vietnamese guides, but we found no recent reviews or contact details, so check its Google Maps reviews before making the trip.
- Esports dates: VALORANT Việt Nam's viewing party for the Champions 2026 grand final is on Sun 18 Oct, 11:00–19:00, at Tây Hồ Gymnasium (101 Xuân La). Every Ticketbox tier showed sold out on 5 Oct, so watch for new releases or watch the final in a Vikings or OEG cyber. LoL Worlds runs 16 Oct–14 Nov in the US, so most matches fall in Hanoi's early morning.
- A Long Biên play day: start at Target Archery (lane 184 Thạch Bàn), then cross to AEON Mall Long Biên for Dream Games bowling and billiards (4th floor, open until midnight at weekends) and the arcades on the 3rd floor. Grab is the easy way over the Red River.
- Billiards and board games: Vietnam won the 2026 three-cushion World Team Championship, and many neighbourhood 'bi-a' halls have pocketless carom tables, so ask for 'bàn lỗ' if you want pool. In board-game cafés most boxes are Vietnamese editions, so stick to language-light games such as Splendor, Dixit or Exploding Kittens.
- How late, really (Oct 2026): Hanoi still runs on a midnight norm. A night-time-economy plan approved in July 2026 would let designated zones (the Old Quarter, Ba Đình, Tây Hồ, Mỹ Đình and others) open until 2am and pilot zones until 6am, but as of August 2026 the zones were still being drawn up. Be inside your last bar before midnight, because walking up to a new door at 1am rarely works. The picks that really serve late are Moon Men (to 1am, 2am Fri–Sun), Nê and NỌT (about 2am) and Puku (24/7).
- Coffee at 3am: NYNA Coffee runs 24/7 at 9 Nguyễn Khắc Cần near the Opera House (also 14TT2A, Vĩnh Tuy), per Znews (Jul 2026). Tống Duy Tân is the all-night strip: Mytour (Sep 2026) lists Puku, Xofa Café & Bistro (No. 14) and Aroi Dessert Café (No. 9) as open 24 hours. Highlands stayed open overnight only for the 2026 World Cup final, so don't count on it.
- Bar-bill set-ups: be wary of a new acquaintance or dating-app match who insists on one particular bar. In a 2023 Tạ Hiện case, women met on Facebook led men to a bar where two of them were billed more than 3 million VND, and police found the women weren't bar staff. Read the menu prices first and pay by the round.
- Karaoke basics: Vietnamese karaoke (KTV) means a private, soundproofed room rented by the hour. You pay per room, not per person, and drinks, snacks and fruit platters are extra. 2026 listings put standard rooms at roughly 100–300k VND an hour (more in the evening, at weekends and for big rooms; luxury KTVs quote about 400k–3m). Agree the room size, hourly rate and any minimum spend at the desk before going upstairs, ask for the English-song tab ('bài tiếng Anh'), and start around 8pm, because karaoke is supposed to stop at midnight.
- Where to sing: family-style KTVs cluster outside the Old Quarter, on Bùi Thị Xuân and Triệu Việt Vương (Hai Bà Trưng), Đào Tấn (Ba Đình), Nghi Tàm (Tây Hồ) and Trần Duy Hưng (Cầu Giấy). The only 2026 evidence for individual venues is listicles, so check recent Google Maps reviews and take a Grab.
- Avoid hostess karaoke ('karaoke tay vịn', or anywhere advertising 'tiếp viên'). Red flags: neon fronts with women outside, no price list and 'up to you' pricing. In Sept 2026 police raided Hanoi-area karaoke parlours and freed 46 trafficked hostesses, some under 16. Never leave your passport as a deposit, and if a bill is disputed, call the police on 113 rather than arguing.
- Apartment-block (tập thể) cafés sit in lived-in buildings: look for the café sign at the foot of the stairwell, climb, and keep your voice down in the shared corridors. Cafés deep in residential lanes often close early out of respect for elderly neighbours.
- The Giảng Võ estate (Ba Đình) makes a local evening: Kẻng Coffee & Tea at 101 B3 Trần Huy Liệu (Znews, Jul 2026), then bia hơi at Nút Quán, 518 D2 Giảng Võ (mia.vn, Jun 2026). Search Maps by name, since tập thể building codes are confusing. Kafeville's site gives the same 101-B3 Trần Huy Liệu address for its Giảng Võ branch, so for Kafeville stick to the Yên Ninh original.
- Triệu Việt Vương (Hai Bà Trưng) is a local café street. Café Thái at No. 27 has roasted its beans over a wood fire since 1926 and is run by the fourth generation, but we found no dated report after Feb 2024, so check recent Google Maps reviews before going.
- Mirage is back: despite the mid-2026 raid, RA lists parties there again from late July 2026, including 26 Sep and 3 Oct (150–270k VND), and Skin On Skin is booked for 13 Nov.
- Unmute's next dates: Carl H (Animals On Psychedelics) on Fri 16 Oct, Organik Festival and Liminal Dream takeovers on Fri 23 Oct, Oil Sound System on Sat 24 Oct, OMOLOKO on Fri 30 Oct, and a three-night anniversary from Thu 5 to Sat 7 Nov 2026 (line-up TBC on RA).
- The National Academy of Music turns 70 with two big nights: 'Resonantia', opera and operetta arias with the Hanoi Symphony Orchestra under Trần Nhật Minh (Sat 24 Oct, 20:00), and a Vietnamese-song gala with Thanh Lam, Mỹ Linh, Quang Hà and Nguyễn Trần Trung Quân (Sat 14 Nov). Venues and tickets were TBA when announced on 30 Sep (VietnamPlus), so watch the Academy's channels and Hanoi Grapevine, and check they're open to the public.
- Karaoke basics: a KTV is a private soundproofed room rented by the hour, so you pay per room, not per person. 2026 listings put standard rooms at roughly 100–300k VND an hour (more at night, at weekends and for big rooms), with beer and fruit platters billed on top. Agree the room size, hourly rate and any minimum spend at the desk, ask for the English-song tab ('bài tiếng Anh') on the touchscreen, and start by about 20:00, because karaoke may not operate between midnight and 08:00.
- Where to sing: family-style KTVs sit outside the Old Quarter, in clusters on Bùi Thị Xuân–Triệu Việt Vương (Hai Bà Trưng), Đào Tấn (Ba Đình) and Nghi Tàm (Tây Hồ). Recent evidence for individual places is thin, so pick one with recent Google Maps reviews and go by Grab.
- Avoid hostess karaoke ('karaoke tay vịn', or places with 'tiếp viên'). Red flags are neon fronts with women outside, no price list and pressure to tip staff; these places are linked to trafficking and padded bills. Never leave your passport as a deposit, and if a bill is disputed, call the police on 113.
- Hanoi still runs on a midnight norm: Old Quarter bars half-lower their shutters around 00:00 and keep serving people already inside, so be in your last bar by midnight. For music after 2am, check RA for Le Maquis, Unmute, Mirage and Otto bar's all-night experimental sessions, which are often free.
- Old guides that send you to Hanoi Rock City (27/52 Tô Ngọc Vân) are out of date: the address is now Valhala Tavern, which soft-opened in September 2026 with DJ, Latin and open-deck nights.

### Hanoi: closed, moved or doubtful

- **Swing Music Lounge**: No first-hand evidence since 2023; only round-ups still list it (re-checked Oct 2026).
- **Trinh Ca Cafe**: No first-hand evidence since 2022; only round-ups still list it (re-checked Oct 2026).
- **Furbrew**: Too doubtful. Its furbrew.vn domain now hosts AI-written filler that calls it a coffee brand, and both Hanoi craft-beer guides updated in Jul and Aug 2026 leave it out. Only an aggregator claims it is open.
- **Kumquat Tree**: Reopened Aug 2025 at 63/52 Tô Ngọc Vân, Tây Hồ (the old 1 Nguyễn Khắc Cần site is closed). No 2026 evidence was found, and it is missing from the Sep 2026 local cocktail guide. Check its Instagram before adding.
- **Cộng Cà Phê – 152D Triệu Việt Vương (2007 original) and 35A Nguyễn Hữu Huân**: Neither branch is on Cộng's official Hanoi store list (branch records updated Mar–May 2026), so treat both as closed. The Cộng pick uses 75 Trích Sài instead, with 27 Nhà Thờ as the central option.
- **Café Nhĩ (2 Hàng Cá)**: No 2025–26 evidence that it still operates.
- **Reng Reng Café (Tập thể A1, 12B Lý Nam Đế)**: Status uncertain, with no 2025–26 evidence found.
- **TOKA Coffee (36 Thi Sách)**: The only evidence is from mid-2025, when the owners denied closure rumours. Loading T and Tamai cover villa cafés with 2026 evidence.
- **Xofa Cafe & Bistro (14 Tống Duy Tân)**: Its 24/7 claim rests only on 2025 listicles and is unverified, and it isn't a strong coffee stop.
- **Acoustic Cafe (236 Hàng Bông)**: Wanderlog marks it permanently closed; only a Dec 2025 listicle (which copies older lists) still includes it.
- **Hanoi Rock City (incl. Wednesday open mic)**: Wound down in 2025: neighbouring demolition damaged the building and shows were cancelled (Vietcetera, Jun 2025), and the lease ended in Dec 2025. The open mic was last documented in 2019.
- **Old Quarter temple shows: Ca trù Thăng Long / Hanoi Ca trù Club (Quan Đế, 28 Hàng Buồm; Kim Ngân, 42–44 Hàng Bạc) and Bạch Mã Temple (76 Hàng Buồm)**: No dated 2025–26 performance schedule found. Only an unreliable 2026 web guide claims nightly 8pm shows, and the Kim Ngân concert found (22 Nov 2025) was a one-off. The temples' Fri–Sun evening openings are in tips; Bích Câu is the verified ca trù pick.
- **Xẩm singing at Đồng Xuân Market (Saturday night market)**: Long-running free street xẩm, but no 2025–26 schedule confirmed; mentioned in tips as a bonus.
- **Tonkin Stories – VICH xẩm show (Kỳ Trà, 124/376 Bưởi)**: Its online calendar still read 'updating' in Apr 2026; couldn't confirm that it runs.
- **Polygon Musik**: Last evidence is a Dec 2025 roundup; no 2026 gig programme found.
- **Totem Vinyl**: No address, and last evidence is from Jan 2024.
- **Hanoi Concert – New Year's Day symphony at the Imperial Citadel**: The 2027 edition hadn't been announced by early Oct 2026.
- **Vietnam Poetry Day (national edition) at Văn Miếu**: Moved: the national Rằm tháng Giêng event no longer stays at Văn Miếu (the research beat traced it to the Imperial Citadel in 2023–24 and Hoa Lư in 2025); the 24th edition opened in Quảng Ninh on 3 Mar 2026 (Nhân Dân), and the 2027 host is unannounced. Hanoi's own Poetry Day at Văn Miếu is listed instead.
- **Nhã Nam Books N' Coffee – 115/D1 Trần Huy Liệu**: No longer on Nhã Nam's official Hanoi store list (research beat, Oct 2026), although older 'best book café' articles still list it; treat as closed.
- **The Bookworm at 44 Châu Long (old address)**: Moved to 44 Phạm Hồng Thái; Bookworm's own site lists Châu Long as the old address, but many map pins still point there.
- **Gác Xép Bookstore (storefront)**: Doubtful storefront: its own site and Znews give three different addresses (Tông Đản, 79C Lý Nam Đế, 81/21 Lý Nam Đế). It works mainly online, so it is in the tips as a delivery option.
- **Planet Laser Game**: Too doubtful. It appears only in SEO listicles (VinWonders, Dec 2025; Mytour, 'updated' 15 Sep 2026), and Mytour's list still includes the closed Laser Pro arena. I found no phone number, fanpage, maps listing or review from 2025–26. Mentioned in tips with a warning to check before going.
- **EZ Gaming (Hàng Da shopping centre)**: Too doubtful. The only source is a GameK feature from Oct 2024, and an Oct 2026 search found no listing, page or review.
- **Laser Pro arena (AEON Mall Long Biên)**: Closed. Per the researchers' notes, it is missing from the mall's store directory and from its 4 Aug 2026 tenant list, and its old hotline now belongs to the mall's kids' attractions.
- **Paintball fields in listicles (Rừng Thông Xanh, Sóc Sơn; 8 Lê Quang Đạo, Mỹ Đình; 79 Võ Chí Công, Tây Hồ)**: Too doubtful. The listicles give no phone numbers or dates, and there is no 2025–26 evidence of open, licensed operation. Paintball guns are regulated in Vietnam.
- **Vikings Thành Thái, Mac Plaza, Khâm Thiên and Nguyễn Tuân**: Closed or moved. None is on Vikings' official branch list (Oct 2026), and GameK reported the Nguyễn Tuân branch closing in 2019.
- **Cu Xa Cafe (Cư Xá Cà Phê)**: Closed: it stopped operating on 31 Aug 2026 after ten years at 209 Tôn Thất Tùng (A11 Khương Thượng block), per Người Quan Sát (11 Sep 2026). The guide already notes the closure in the 30S Coffea pick and the café tips.
- **Café Thái (since 1926)**: Unverified: no dated coverage newer than VietNam News (Feb 2024) turned up. The Tuổi Trẻ News (Oct 2020) and Người Quan Sát features are older, and an undated guide (flavorsofhanoi.com) lists 6:00–22:00. Round 1 dropped it for the same reason. It's named in the tips with a check-first note; add it if a 2026 visit confirms it, since 2026 is its centenary.
- **The First Coffee & Cocktail**: Doubtful: the only evidence is a Feb 2025 Xanh SM listing (18:00–02:00, drinks 40–100k VND), and nothing from 2026 turned up, so it's left out of the picks and the tips.
- **Sanctuary Art & Drinks**: No RA-listed nights at all between May and early October 2026 (latest evidence Feb 2026); couldn't confirm it still runs club nights.
- **Tôm Karaoke (116 Bùi Thị Xuân)**: Its Foody listing isn't marked closed, but the latest dated review is from July 2019 and newer mentions are only SEO listicles, so couldn't confirm it operates in 2025–26. No other foreigner-friendly KTV could be verified (the chains with online booking and published prices, ICOOL, Nnice and Kingdom, are Saigon-only), so karaoke advice went into tips.
- **Xẩm in the Old Quarter (VICH heritage house, 9 Chân Cầm)**: Latest evidence is from May 2024; couldn't confirm it still runs.

### Hanoi: considered but not picked

- **Turtle Lake Brewing Company (TLBC)**: Status unconfirmed. The only first-hand visit in the guides dates from Dec 2019, and it is missing from the Aug 2026 local craft-beer guide.
- **angelina (Sofitel Legend Metropole)**: Two duplicate entries merged, then folded into the Bamboo Bar pick (same hotel). Its daily 4–7pm 'angelina hour' (second drink free) is in that tip.
- **Pool Terrace & Bar (Fairmont Hanoi)**: Not selected. It opened in mid-2026 with cocktails from 450k++, and the detailed review was written in partnership with the hotel. Terraço covers the Hoàn Kiếm Lake view for less.
- **Moonlight Sky Bar**: Not selected. A solid mid-range Old Quarter hotel rooftop (open 2026), but less distinctive than Terraço and The Summit.
- **Lighthouse Sky Bar**: Not selected. Same niche as Moonlight (a La Siesta hotel rooftop, open 2026); Terraço and The Summit are stronger.
- **Bouchon Wine Bar**: Not selected for space. Open per the Sep 2026 guide; named in the tips with its 2-for-1 wine happy hour.
- **Cork & Bottle**: Not selected for space. Open per the Sep 2026 guide; named in the tips with its 2-for-1 wine happy hour.
- **Pasteur Street Brewing Co. – Au Trieu Taproom**: Not selected: a taproom of a Saigon brewery chain (open per its 2026 website). Named in the tips as the central craft-beer option.
- **7 Bridges Brewing Co. – Tay Ho**: Not selected: the Hanoi taproom of a Đà Nẵng brewery. Named in the tips.
- **Hoa Vien Craft Brewery**: Not selected. A Czech-style beer hall, less distinctive than the picks.
- **The Bottle Shop**: Not selected. More a craft-beer shop than a bar destination.
- **Hanoi Cider Co.**: Not selected: less distinctive. Its older 9 Xuân Diệu address is out of date (now 278 Nghi Tàm).
- **Lang Thang Cocktail Bar**: Not selected (likely open per the Sep 2026 guide). Named in the tips for a Trúc Bạch crawl.
- **Bia Hoi Hai Xom**: Not selected. A chain of large beer halls, mostly outside the centre, with evidence only from roundups; Lan Chín is the central local pick. Its 22 Tăng Bạt Hổ branch is no longer listed.
- **Bia Van Ho**: Not selected. Evidence comes only from roundups, and sources disagree on the address (2B Lê Đại Hành vs 2B Hoa Lư).
- **Tong Duy Tan Food Street**: A late-night food street rather than a bar; mentioned in the Nê tip.
- **GoodTime Burger**: Not selected. A burger bar whose draw is food and DJ sets rather than drinks; better in the music section (clubs beat).
- **Otto bar**: Music-led (experimental, noise and electronic nights with short films), so it is better in the music section. No hours or prices were published.
- **XOAY**: No street address was published, and its draw is Saturday DJ parties rather than drinks; better in the music section once an address is found.
- **Hanoi Train Street cafés (Phố cà phê đường tàu)**: Left out on purpose. It's a spectacle, not a coffee stop, and the trackside cafés operate illegally in the rail safety corridor. Tour companies have been barred since Mar 2025, and police clear the tracks periodically. A safety note is in the tips instead.
- **Café Thái 1926 (27 Triệu Việt Vương)**: The latest dated coverage found is from Feb 2024 (VietNam News), and 2025–26 operation could not be confirmed.
- **Bancong Cafe (2 Đinh Liệt)**: Only 2025 listicle evidence. Phố Cổ, Đinh and Miên Man cover balcony and view seats with stronger evidence.
- **Café Nhân (39D Hàng Hành)**: Not selected. Tatler (Aug 2025) shows it open, but it's a plain pavement phin café and Café Lâm covers that experience. It's mentioned in the crawl tip instead.
- **All Day Coffee (55 Hàng Bún / 37 Quang Trung)**: Not selected. It's a pleasant modern egg and salted-egg coffee café, but pricier and less distinctive than Giảng, Đinh and Loading T.
- **ACID8 Specialty Coffee (34 Xóm Hà Hồi)**: Not a separate pick. It was confirmed in Jan 2026 and is folded into VULAB's tip, a few doors along the same lane.
- **Blackbird Coffee (5 Chân Cầm)**: Not a separate pick. It was confirmed in Jan 2026 and is named as the fallback in Loading T's tip, across the street.
- **Capella Coffee Roaster (Tây Hồ)**: Not selected. It was confirmed in Feb 2026, but it's a 20–30 minute ride from the Old Quarter and the chosen roasters outclass it.
- **Ta Cà Phê (4A Ngõ Gạch)**: Not selected. It's a solid seed-to-cup roaster, confirmed on its own 2026 site, but it closes at 18:00 and overlaps with RAAW. It's mentioned in the tips.
- **3C Roastery**: Not selected. Its DIY pour-over counter is fun, but it's a step below the chosen roasters, and its address comes only from Vietcetera.
- **Still Color Cafe (32 ngõ Thọ Xương)**: Not selected. It was confirmed in Jan 2026, but it's a tiny minimalist bar with a few chairs, a minor stop next to refined. and Loading T.
- **PIN Cafe'n Clothes (33 Hàng Hòm)**: Not selected. It's a quirky pour-over counter inside a clothing boutique, but only a minor stop.
- **Manzi Art Space (14 Phan Huy Ích)**: Its draw is occasional ticketed concerts, which fits the music section better. Café hours and prices were not found.
- **HUM (Hanoi Underground Movement)**: No public address (Instagram only); its nights also appear at other venues, e.g. Mirage in Jun 2026.
- **BIGBANG 2026–2027 World Tour <XX: COSMOS> – Hanoi (24–25 Oct 2026)**: Both nights at Mỹ Đình Stadium sold out in August; noted in tips.
- **Làng Tôi – My Village (Lune Production)**: A bamboo-circus show more than a music venue. The official site confirms a Hanoi run from 3 Nov 2026, but the new venue (reported as 42 Tràng Tiền, under renovation) couldn't be re-confirmed; noted in tips.
- **Library Lounge (La Siesta Classic Hàng Thùng)**: Not selected: a pleasant read-and-drink hotel lounge with 2,000+ books and happy hour 16:00–18:00 (Znews, Sept 2026), but Tender does books and cocktails better; it is in the tips as the end of the book loop.
- **The Wiselands**: Not selected: a library-café with about 3,000 mostly Vietnamese titles on Hạ Hồi lane, open 7:00–23:00 (Znews, Aug 2026); kept as a stop on the French Quarter book loop in the tips.
- **Thanh Ninh Books**: Not selected: a promising independent bookshop-café opened in early 2026 at 88 Nguyễn Du (Znews, Aug 2026), but it has only that one source and Vietnamese-language stock; mentioned in the tips.
- **Matca 'Read with Us' photobook corner (Sóng Sánh Café)**: Not selected: a niche photobook showcase (edition 16 runs to 31 Dec 2026, Hanoi Grapevine); noted in the tips for photo-book fans.
- **Meet the author: Đoan Bùi at Kim Đồng Publishing House**: Not selected: a one-off talk on Sun 11 Oct 2026 at 15:00 (free, registration via the Institut français); noted in the tips.
- **Nha Nam Books N' Coffee (IPH Xuân Thủy)**: Not selected: far out in Cầu Giấy; Nhã Nam's stall on Hanoi Book Street is the central way to browse its catalogue.
- **Tu Sach Coffee**: Not selected: a second-hand Vietnamese book café in the Giảng Võ housing estate (Znews, Aug 2026), but it is out of the way, Vietnamese-only and backed by a single source; Tranquil covers the book café better.
- **Seen Escape**: Not selected: it is open (official site, 2025 price list), but its storylines and puzzles are Vietnamese-only. Miss Terry is the English-friendly escape-room pick.
- **Minipolo – The Board Game Café**: Not selected: it is still listed (Boardgame.vn, Mar 2026) and hosts student tournaments, but The Root fills the board-game slot more centrally. Minipolo is named in The Root's tip.
- **City Games Plus Royal City**: Not selected: a new (Sep 2026), family-oriented card arcade with a VR jeep and space-ride simulators but no competitive shooters. Timezone already covers arcades and VR.
- **VALORANT Champions 2026 Grand Final Viewing Party**: Not selected: a one-off event on 18 Oct 2026, and every Ticketbox tier showed sold out on 5 Oct. Covered in tips.
- **Airsoft / BB-gun fields advertised on social media**: Avoid. No legal operating venue was found, and Vietnam treats many air guns as weapons (Dân trí, 16 May 2026).
- **Hoa Vien Czech Brewery (Hoa Viên, 1A Tăng Bạt Hổ)**: Not selected: it's open (its site promoted a 2026 World Cup final screening on 19 Jul 2026 and lists only 1A Tăng Bạt Hổ in Hanoi), but it's a big Czech-style beer restaurant where groups come for dry-aged steak (348k VND per 100 g), and round 1 already judged it less distinctive than the picks. Mytour (Sep 2026) still lists a 10 Nguyễn Biểu branch that the official site omits.
- **NYNA Coffee (Nguyễn Khắc Cần)**: Not selected: a 24/7 coffee, tea and pastry chain (9 Nguyễn Khắc Cần near the Opera House; 24/7 per Znews, 18 Jul 2026). It's useful but not a destination, so it's in the tips as the 3am coffee stop.
- **Nut Quan – Bia Hoi Ha Noi (Giang Vo)**: Not selected: a single 2026 listicle source (mia.vn, Jun 2026) with little detail beyond 'consistent quality'; Vân Hồ and Cường Hói are stronger local bia hơi picks. It's in the Giảng Võ tip.
- **PaiOi Cafe**: Not selected: famous for a seasonal affogato (launched 17 Oct 2025, 75k VND, capped at 100 cups a day), but the only evidence is a Nov 2025 Znews report, no street number on Trần Quốc Toản was found, and it's unclear whether the special returns for autumn 2026.
- **Treeving Coffee**: Not selected: its only draw is the view back across the Red River to Long Biên Bridge, which Serein and Hỏa Xa cover from the Old Quarter side, and the evidence is 2025 listings only (Xanh SM, mia.vn).
- **1900 Le Théâtre**: Not selected: a commercial house/EDM superclub with bottle service, a weak fit for this guide. It had no RA-listed nights from May to early Oct 2026, and the latest dated evidence found is DJ Mag's 2024 ranking.
- **'70 Years – Sounds That Linger' gala with Thanh Lam and Mỹ Linh (14 Nov 2026)**: Time, venue and tickets were still TBA at the 30 Sep 2026 announcement (VietnamPlus), so it can't be booked yet; noted in tips.
- **'Resonantia' opera-aria gala (24 Oct 2026)**: Venue and tickets were TBA at the 30 Sep 2026 announcement (VietnamPlus); noted in tips.
- **BIGBANG 2026–2027 World Tour 'XX: COSMOS' in Hanoi**: Not selected: already in the guide (sold out; covered in tips and dropped).
- **Hào Nam street instrument shops**: Not selected: only undated listicle evidence (Toplist) and no opening hours; couldn't confirm individual shops for 2025–26.
- **Hero Bar**: Not selected: only one RA-listed night in 2026 (3 Jul); events are irregular.
- **Manzi Art Space**: Not selected: concerts are occasional. The latest found was on 27 Mar 2026, and nothing is announced from October 2026.
- **SiaM – Schubert in a Mug**: Not selected: already in the guide's tips, and no volume has been announced since Vol. 46 (Jan 2026).
- **Vietnam National Academy of Music – Grand Concert Hall**: Not selected: already covered in the guide's tips (VNSO season at 77 Hào Nam); Oct 2026–Jan 2027 programmes not posted.

## Ninh Bình

*Ninh Bình · karst and rice-field country, about 2 hours south of Hanoi*

Ninh Bình, two hours south of Hanoi, is karst-and-paddy country where the best evenings come in two halves: sunset drinks on a rooftop facing Hang Múa or the Tam Cốc wharf, then a 20-minute Grab into the city for free chèo and xẩm, the Anh Hùng Cờ Lau show and cocktails beside lantern-lit Kỳ Lân lake. Base yourself in Tam Cốc for garden cafés, bike lanes and the hostel-bar crowd, or in the city if you'd rather walk home from the shows. Readers get carved poems, the 1010 edict and Graham Greene's Phát Diệm instead of bookshops, while gaming is thin: pool, go-karts and archery rather than arcades.

### Ninh Bình: Cafés (Cà phê)

Tam Cốc has the province's densest café scene: family-run garden cafés tucked between paddies, lotus ponds and karst towers that do egg, coconut and salt coffee well, plus a couple of places that take espresso seriously (At Where in Tam Cốc, The Lim in the city). The famous golden rice belongs to late May and early June, so from October to March choose cafés for river, lake and karst views, and note that most close by 21:00–22:00.

#### Cosy & specialty cafés

- **★ At Where** · Tam Cốc (Đam Khê Ngoài lanes, south of Tam Cốc lake) · *checked 2026*
  - Tam Cốc's standout café is a plant-filled, carefully designed room run by a friendly English-speaking couple, with artisanal coffee, excellent matcha and pastries that are genuinely good (a buttery pain au chocolat, Basque cheesecake). After dark the lights go down and it becomes a cosy wine bar with an international list and a cheese board.
  - Tip: Go twice: in the morning for the Le Creme coffee or the Green Cloud or coconut matcha with a pastry, and after dinner for a glass of wine and the cheese board, when reviewers say it's calm enough to read. Si Café and Axasola are a couple of minutes' walk away in the same lanes.
  - Hours: Daily 9:00–23:00 (Google)
  - Address: Tam Cốc, phường Nam Hoa Lư (search 'At Where' on Google Maps)
  - Price: No prices published online
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=At%20Where%2C%20Tam%20C%E1%BB%91c%2C%20Nam%20Hoa%20L%C6%B0%2C%20Ninh%20B%C3%ACnh) · [Source](https://wanderlog.com/place/details/11501002)
- **★ Brick Coffee Shop** · Tam Cốc (road ĐT491C, east of the boat wharf towards Hang Múa) · $ · *likely open*
  - Tam Cốc's long-running favourite (4.9 from about 1,670 Google reviews) is a two-storey café of bare brick and wood run by a friendly couple, with a wall of pottery made by the owner's brother and cakes baked in-house. Its egg coffee is less sweet than Hanoi's.
  - Tip: Pair an egg coffee or iced salted coffee with carrot cake or banana bread with passion-fruit jam, and sit upstairs for the view, fast Wi-Fi and sockets. It's a good first stop on a bike ride to Hang Múa, but it shuts at 21:00.
  - Hours: Daily 7:00–21:00 (Google)
  - Address: ĐT491C, Ninh Thắng, phường Nam Hoa Lư
  - Price: Drinks about 25–55k VND (2025 listing)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Brick%20Coffee%20Shop%2C%20%C4%90T491C%2C%20Nam%20Hoa%20L%C6%B0%2C%20Ninh%20B%C3%ACnh) · [Source](https://wanderlog.com/place/details/1122570/coffee-shop)
- **Xanh Coffee** · Tam Cốc (Văn Lâm backstreets) · $ · *likely open*
  - A two-storey family café hidden in Tam Cốc's backstreets, wrapped in plants around a small pond, with a perfect 5.0 from more than 1,000 Google reviews. The egg coffee is rich and chocolatey and the coconut coffee is a favourite.
  - Tip: Cash only (with free bottled water) and closed on Sundays. It also does a cheap breakfast: smoothie bowls, oat porridge and pho.
  - Hours: Mon–Fri 8:00–21:00, Sat 8:00–20:00, closed Sun (Google)
  - Address: Ngõ Hòa Bình 1, TDP Văn Lâm, phường Nam Hoa Lư
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Xanh%20Coffee%2C%20Ng%C3%B5%20H%C3%B2a%20B%C3%ACnh%201%2C%20V%C4%83n%20L%C3%A2m%2C%20Nam%20Hoa%20L%C6%B0%2C%20Ninh%20B%C3%ACnh) · [Source](https://wanderlog.com/place/details/5835970/xanh-coffee)
- **The Lim Coffee** · Ninh Bình city centre (Nguyễn Huệ) · $ · *likely open*
  - One of the city's best bets for a proper espresso: a retro-meets-modern café whose coffee is good enough that visitors buy bags of its beans to take home, with friendly staff, air-con, good Wi-Fi and plenty of sockets.
  - Tip: Order an espresso or iced Americano, take a ground-floor window seat and ask about beans to take away. It's the coffee stop for a day in the city before an evening at Phố cổ Hoa Lư.
  - Hours: Daily 7:00–23:00 (Google)
  - Address: 3 Nguyễn Huệ, phường Hoa Lư
  - Price: Drinks about 25–40k VND (2025 listing)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=The%20Lim%20Coffee%2C%203%20Nguy%E1%BB%85n%20Hu%E1%BB%87%2C%20Hoa%20L%C6%B0%2C%20Ninh%20B%C3%ACnh) · [Source](https://wanderlog.com/place/details/8767765)
- **Si Café** · Tam Cốc (Đam Khê Ngoài lanes, south of Tam Cốc lake) · *likely open*
  - A small vintage-styled café in a quiet country lane, run by friendly local owners and rated a perfect 5.0 from more than 280 Google reviews, with carefully made egg coffee, egg cream and espresso drinks.
  - Tip: Order the egg coffee or egg cream, or a latte if you want espresso. There's bike parking, so it's an easy first stop on a ride to Bích Động; At Where and Axasola are in the same lanes.
  - Hours: Daily 8:00–22:00 (Google)
  - Address: Đam Khê Ngoài (Plus Code 6W7M+PC7), phường Nam Hoa Lư
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Si%20Caf%C3%A9%2C%20%C4%90am%20Kh%C3%AA%20Ngo%C3%A0i%2C%20Nam%20Hoa%20L%C6%B0%2C%20Ninh%20B%C3%ACnh) · [Source](https://wanderlog.com/place/details/14233722)

#### Rice-field & karst-view cafés

- **An Tam Coc Restaurant & Coffee** · Tam Cốc (Văn Lâm, by the Ngô Đồng river) · $ · *likely open*
  - A family café-restaurant on the Ngô Đồng river whose terrace looks across rice fields to the karsts, praised as much for its coffee (salt coffee, and drinks sweetened with honey rather than syrup) as for its generous Vietnamese cooking. It holds 4.8 from nearly 2,200 Google reviews.
  - Tip: Come between meals for a quiet coffee on the terrace or the calmer second floor, since it fills with diners at lunch and dinner; vegetarian dishes are available. From October to March the fields are green or fallow; the famous golden rice comes in late May and early June.
  - Hours: Daily 8:00–22:00, Wed to 21:30 (Google)
  - Address: Đội 4, khu Văn Lâm, Tam Cốc road, phường Nam Hoa Lư
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=An%20Tam%20Coc%20Restaurant%20%26%20Coffee%2C%20V%C4%83n%20L%C3%A2m%2C%20Tam%20C%E1%BB%91c%2C%20Nam%20Hoa%20L%C6%B0%2C%20Ninh%20B%C3%ACnh) · [Source](https://wanderlog.com/place/details/5644968/an-tam-coc-restaurant--coffee)
- **Coffee Cat** · Tam Cốc (Văn Lâm, just off the main road) · *likely open*
  - A calm café off the main road where resident cats curl up on laps while you look out over a lake to the limestone hills (4.8 from about 900 Google reviews). The egg coffee has a notably thick cream and the coconut coffee tastes properly of coconut.
  - Tip: Bring a book for a slow afternoon; oat and soy milk are available, and there's simple Vietnamese food such as mushroom pho and spring rolls. Skip it if you're allergic to cats.
  - Hours: Daily 7:00–21:00 (Google)
  - Address: Văn Lâm, phường Nam Hoa Lư
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Coffee%20Cat%2C%20V%C4%83n%20L%C3%A2m%2C%20Tam%20C%E1%BB%91c%2C%20Nam%20Hoa%20L%C6%B0%2C%20Ninh%20B%C3%ACnh) · [Source](https://wanderlog.com/place/details/8316866)
- **La Cactus Sky Café** · Bái Đính area (Tây Hoa Lư, west of Tràng An) · *likely open*
  - A cactus-filled, Indochine-styled café whose rooftop looks straight across to the Bái Đính pagoda complex, rated a perfect 5.0 on Google, with English-speaking staff and good coconut coffee, salted-cream coffee and matcha.
  - Tip: Pair it with Bái Đính: come early in the morning for quiet, or stop for a pho lunch. Staff can help with bus tickets.
  - Address: Xóm 5, phường Tây Hoa Lư
  - Price: Reviewers call prices reasonable
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=La%20Cactus%20Sky%20Caf%C3%A9%2C%20X%C3%B3m%205%2C%20T%C3%A2y%20Hoa%20L%C6%B0%2C%20Ninh%20B%C3%ACnh) · [Source](https://wanderlog.com/place/details/18916857)

### Ninh Bình: Bars (Quán bar)

Nightlife is low-key and early. Tam Cốc's bars sit within a few hundred metres of the boat wharf: a pool-side beer garden, two hostel rooftops facing the karsts and a craft-beer pub, most closing by 22:00–23:30 (At Where, listed under Cafés, becomes a wine bar until 23:00). For cocktails head into the city to Ume beside Phố cổ Hoa Lư, and for a local drink order rượu Kim Sơn, the province's rice spirit, with a goat dinner.

#### Beer gardens & craft beer

- **★ Chookie's Beer Garden** · Tam Cốc · $$ · *checked 2026*
  - Tam Cốc's original traveller hangout: an Australian–Vietnamese couple started Chookie's in Ninh Bình in 2014 and opened this garden in 2017, with booths around a shallow pool under pomelo and mango trees, cold Vietnamese craft beer, wood-fired pizza and Aussie burgers, and warm lights strung up after dark.
  - Tip: Come straight off the boat or a bike loop around sunset and grab a pool-side booth; the hibiscus cider makes a change from beer. It shuts at about 22:00, so move on to the wharf afterwards. The same team runs the Sunset Bar rooftop and The Laneway at Hang Múa.
  - Hours: Daily 9:00–22:00 (Google; its own site says from 10:00)
  - Address: Tam Cốc Rd, Ninh Hải, phường Nam Hoa Lư
  - Price: Google price level moderate; no drink prices published online
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Chookie%27s%20Beer%20Garden%2C%20Tam%20C%E1%BB%91c%20Rd%2C%20Nam%20Hoa%20L%C6%B0%2C%20Ninh%20B%C3%ACnh) · [Source](https://chookiestravel.com/chookies-beer-garden/)
- **Linh Chi Craft Beer Pub** · Tam Cốc · $ · *likely open*
  - Tam Cốc's best bet for Vietnamese craft beer is a relaxed pub-restaurant with a broad list of local craft labels, 10k VND local draft in the evening and cheap, good Vietnamese food (4.7 from about 650 Google reviews).
  - Tip: Reviewers single out the passion-fruit IPA and the jasmine IPA; eat summer rolls, papaya salad or rare goat with them. It's one of Tam Cốc's later closers, at 23:30.
  - Hours: Daily 7:00–23:30 (Google)
  - Address: Phường Nam Hoa Lư (Tam Cốc)
  - Price: Local draft 10k VND from 17:00; craft beers cost more
