  - Price: Free to browse; second-hand books from about 20k VND; book-café drinks about 25–60k VND
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=%C4%90%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Dng%20s%C3%A1ch%20TP.HCM%2C%20Nguy%E1%BB%85n%20V%C4%83n%20B%C3%ACnh%2C%20S%C3%A0i%20G%C3%B2n%20ward%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://tuoitre.vn/10-nam-hoat-dong-duong-sach-tp-hcm-thu-tren-433-ti-dong-20260108141811215.htm)
- **★ Pages of Passion** · District 1 (Bến Thành ward) · $$ · *checked 2026*
  - Saigon's most talked-about English-language bookshop, run by a Hanoian couple who began selling online in 2024 and now fill a sixth-floor apartment with new and gently used fiction, poetry, philosophy and history. Its Vietnam shelf of diaspora and translated writers (Nguyễn Phan Quế Mai, Ocean Vuong, Thi Bui) is a highlight, and a quiet reading café makes it easy to linger.
  - Tip: It's inside an apartment building, so find the entrance of no. 35 and take the lift to level 6 rather than hunting for a shopfront. Ask owners Thảo and Huy for a recommendation, and follow @pagesofpassionvn on Instagram for talks and workshops (it hosted an English poetry night in 2025).
  - Hours: 9:00–22:00
  - Address: Level 6, 35 Nguyễn Văn Tráng, Bến Thành ward (District 1)
  - Price: Books about 140–660k VND (new and used); drinks from 50k VND
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Pages%20of%20Passion%2C%2035%20Nguy%E1%BB%85n%20V%C4%83n%20Tr%C3%A1ng%2C%20B%E1%BA%BFn%20Th%C3%A0nh%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://pagesofpassion.vn/)
- **Bá Tân Used Books** (Bá Tân – Sách cũ Thư viện) · District 3 (alley 451 Hai Bà Trưng, near Tân Định) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - Former history teacher Lê Bá Tân runs one of Saigon's best-loved used-book houses: books sold by the kilo crowd the alley entrance, while calmer rooms inside hold near-new titles, manga, philosophy and rare Vietnamese first editions. Free tea and coffee, resident cats and a free-to-read shelf make it feel like a readers' home.
  - Tip: The stock is mostly Vietnamese, so come for the atmosphere and collectible editions, and ask staff about old Saigon-era titles. Then walk on to Emtrangtri at 451/50 in the same alley for thrifted English and French books.
  - Hours: 8:00–21:30
  - Address: 451/22 Hai Bà Trưng (inside the alley), Xuân Hòa ward (District 3)
  - Price: By the kilo about 40–60k VND/kg; recent titles typically 40–60% of cover price; rare editions priced individually
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=B%C3%A1%20T%C3%A2n%20S%C3%A1ch%20c%C5%A9%20Th%C6%B0%20vi%E1%BB%87n%2C%20451/22%20Hai%20B%C3%A0%20Tr%C6%B0ng%2C%20Xu%C3%A2n%20H%C3%B2a%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://saigoneer.com/in-plain-sight/26671-at-b%C3%A1-t%C3%A2n-bookstore,-a-home-for-vintage-books,-readers,-and-goodness)
- **InBook** · District 1 (Sài Gòn ward) · $$$ · *checked 2026*
  - Founded by a French-to-Vietnamese translator, this two-floor shop above a stylish café sells new French and English books (literary classics, crime, YA, romance and children's titles) plus Vietnamese writing in translation. A reading corner with free drinks and a 'storekeeper's picks' shelf of handwritten reviews make it feel like a friend's library.
  - Tip: Come for the French shelves, which are rare in Saigon, and read the handwritten staff reviews before you choose. Orders over 500k VND get free delivery within HCMC, so you needn't carry your haul around.
  - Hours: Daily 9:00–20:00
  - Address: 25A Nguyễn Bỉnh Khiêm, Sài Gòn ward (District 1)
  - Price: English paperbacks about 145–475k VND (classics from ~145k; most new fiction 300–400k)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=InBook%2C%2025A%20Nguy%E1%BB%85n%20B%E1%BB%89nh%20Khi%C3%AAm%2C%20S%C3%A0i%20G%C3%B2n%20ward%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://inbook.vn/)
- **Emtrangtri Bookstore** · District 3 (alley 451 Hai Bà Trưng, near Tân Định) · *checked 2026*
  - A one-room bookshop in the living room of a house deep in alley 451, selling English, French and Vietnamese books the owner thrifts personally, with an offbeat selection you won't find in the chains. Armchairs and sofas invite you to sit and read.
  - Tip: It's home-based with no published hours, so check @_emtrangtri on Instagram or Threads before you go, and pair it with Bá Tân at 451/22 in the same alley.
  - Address: 451/50 Hai Bà Trưng, Xuân Hòa ward (District 3)
  - Price: No price list published; Saigoneer calls prices moderate to high
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Emtrangtri%20Bookstore%2C%20451/50%20Hai%20B%C3%A0%20Tr%C6%B0ng%2C%20Xu%C3%A2n%20H%C3%B2a%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://saigoneer.com/vietnam-literature/29211-5-indie-bookstores-in-saigon-for-those-who-are-addicted-to-the-scent-of-paper)
- **Trần Nhân Tôn Second-hand Book Street** (Phố sách cũ Trần Nhân Tôn) · District 5 (Chợ Quán / An Đông wards) · $ · *checked 2025*
  - The city's densest cluster of used bookshops: around ten family-run stores such as A Hoàng (a pushcart after 1975, a fixed shop since 1994), Tấn Phương, Phước Hậu and Khải Vinh. Their piles of old editions, comics, dictionaries and textbooks hide rare titles at low prices.
  - Tip: Nearly everything is in Vietnamese, so bring titles or author names written down and ask for 'sách xưa' (old editions). Several owners are collectors themselves and post new arrivals on social media.
  - Hours: Roughly 6:00–22:00 (varies by shop)
  - Address: Trần Nhân Tôn street, Chợ Quán and An Đông wards (District 5)
  - Price: Everyday used books are cheap; rare and antiquarian editions are priced individually
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Ph%E1%BB%91%20s%C3%A1ch%20c%C5%A9%20Tr%E1%BA%A7n%20Nh%C3%A2n%20T%C3%B4n%2C%20Ch%E1%BB%A3%20Qu%C3%A1n%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://tuoitre.vn/ky-uc-sai-gon-xua-trong-nhung-tiem-sach-cu-2025100508564539.htm)

#### Literary landmarks

- **★ Hotel Continental Saigon** (Khách sạn Continental Sài Gòn) · District 1 (Lam Sơn Square, opposite the Opera House) · *checked 2026*
  - Graham Greene was a long-term guest in room 214 of this 1880 hotel, where he conceived The Quiet American, and its terrace was 'Radio Catinat', the rumour exchange of the French war. In the American war, correspondents drank at its ground-floor 'Continental Shelf' bar while Time and Newsweek kept their bureaux upstairs.
  - Tip: Take an evening drink at Le Jardin, the hotel's garden cocktail bar, or eat at Le Bourgeois, which the hotel bills as Greene's favourite rendezvous. Then step into Lam Sơn Square and look up at corner room 214: the square below is where the novel's car bomb goes off.
  - Hours: Hotel open 24h; bar and restaurant hours not published online
  - Address: 132–134 Đồng Khởi, Sài Gòn ward (District 1)
  - Price: Free to admire from the square; drinks and meals at hotel prices (menus not published online)
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Hotel%20Continental%20Saigon%2C%20132%20%C4%90%E1%BB%93ng%20Kh%E1%BB%9Fi%2C%20S%C3%A0i%20G%C3%B2n%20ward%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://continentalsaigon.com/nha-hang-bar)
- **★ The Quiet American walk: rue Catinat (Đồng Khởi)** (Đường Đồng Khởi (rue Catinat, Tự Do)) · District 1 (Đồng Khởi, from Notre-Dame down to the Saigon River) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - Rue Catinat, today's Đồng Khởi, is the spine of Greene's novel, and the buildings he used still line it: 164 Đồng Khởi (the old Sûreté, Inspector Vigot's office), the Continental, and the Grand Hotel at no. 8, model for Fowler's 'room over the rue Catinat'. It ends at the Majestic by the river, where Fowler takes his 7pm 'cocktail time' in the cool wind off the Saigon River.
  - Tip: Start around 5pm at the Notre-Dame end and finish at M Bar on the Majestic's roof. Detours: the Caravelle's rooftop Saigon Saigon bar (the war correspondents' hangout, reopened 28 Sep 2026); the Rex at 141 Nguyễn Huệ, where the US command's daily briefings were mocked as the 'Five O'Clock Follies'; and 39 Hàm Nghi, the first US embassy and Pyle's 'American Legation'. Givral, Phượng's milk bar, was demolished in 2009, and the old Sûreté block has long been earmarked for redevelopment.
  - Hours: Any time; best from about 5pm
  - Address: Start at the Notre-Dame end of Đồng Khởi; finish at the Hotel Majestic, 1 Đồng Khởi
  - Price: Free; budget for a drink or two at the hotel bars
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=%C4%90%E1%BB%93ng%20Kh%E1%BB%9Fi%20Street%2C%20S%C3%A0i%20G%C3%B2n%20ward%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://saigoneer.com/saigon-heritage/1529-looking-back-at-graham-greene-s-saigon)
- **Marguerite Duras's Saigon (The Lover trail)** (Sài Gòn của Marguerite Duras (Người tình)) · District 3 to District 5 (Chợ Lớn) · $ · *likely open*
  - Duras was born in Gia Định in 1914 and studied at the Lycée Chasseloup-Laubat, which survives as Lê Quý Đôn High School (founded 1874) with its French lycée buildings intact. Nearby, the colonial villa at 141 Võ Văn Tần was her family's home from 1932 until they left Indochina in 1933, just after the affair she turned into L'Amant.
  - Tip: Both are working buildings (the villa was a bank office when last documented), so admire them from the street. In District 5, the former Lycée Pétrus Ký (now Lê Hồng Phong High School, 235 Nguyễn Văn Cừ) stood in for the school in the 1992 film. For the real lover's house, make a day trip to Sa Đéc: Huỳnh Thủy Lê's 1895 family home (255A Nguyễn Huệ) is now a national heritage site where guides retell the romance, with lunch and overnight stays on offer.
  - Hours: Exteriors only; no public visiting arrangements
  - Address: Start: Lê Quý Đôn High School (ex-Lycée Chasseloup-Laubat), 110 Nguyễn Thị Minh Khai, Xuân Hòa ward (District 3)
  - Price: Free from the street; Sa Đéc house entry fee not confirmed online
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Tr%C6%B0%E1%BB%9Dng%20THPT%20L%C3%AA%20Qu%C3%BD%20%C4%90%C3%B4n%2C%20110%20Nguy%E1%BB%85n%20Th%E1%BB%8B%20Minh%20Khai%2C%20Xu%C3%A2n%20H%C3%B2a%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://saigoneer.com/vietnam-heritage/6595-saigon-on-the-silver-screen-the-lover,-1992)

#### Book cafés & libraries

- **General Sciences Library of Ho Chi Minh City** (Thư viện Khoa học Tổng hợp TP.HCM) · District 1 (Bến Thành ward, beside the HCMC Museum) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - One of Vietnam's oldest public libraries fills the early-1970s modernist former National Library by architects Bùi Quang Hạnh and Nguyễn Hữu Thiện, all concrete louvres and a 14-storey book tower, on the site of the colonial Khám Lớn prison. Its 600,000-plus titles include foreign-language literature, the 71-metre day-lit reading room has long wooden tables, and stone benches sit under century-old trees outside.
  - Tip: Reading rooms need a reader card: 40k VND a year for adults (16+), issued at the desk with your passport and a photo taken on the spot, or pre-register at phucvu.thuvientphcm.gov.vn/Patron/Register. Remember it's closed on Fridays; the Hán-Nôm room of classical-script books keeps shorter hours.
  - Hours: Mon–Thu 7:30–19:00, Sat–Sun 9:00–17:00; closed Fridays and public holidays
  - Address: 69 Lý Tự Trọng, Bến Thành ward (District 1)
  - Price: Adult reader card 40k VND/year; free for over-60s
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=Th%C6%B0%20vi%E1%BB%87n%20Khoa%20h%E1%BB%8Dc%20T%E1%BB%95ng%20h%E1%BB%A3p%20TP.HCM%2C%2069%20L%C3%BD%20T%E1%BB%B1%20Tr%E1%BB%8Dng%2C%20B%E1%BA%BFn%20Th%C3%A0nh%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://thuvientphcm.gov.vn/phuc-vu-ban-doc/dich-vu-cap-the-nguoi-lon)
- **Bản Cà Phê (book café)** (Bản Cà Phê) · District 1 (Lê Lợi, Sài Gòn ward) · $ · *checked 2026*
  - A Japanese-styled wooden book café in the heart of District 1 with more than 1,000 books, a big manga and anime shelf and a mezzanine of floor seating for quiet reading.
  - Tip: Climb to the mezzanine floor seats for the quietest corner. It's an easy escape from the Bến Thành and Nguyễn Huệ crowds, and it stays open late.
  - Hours: 8:00–22:00 (some listings say 21:00)
  - Address: 38/7 Lê Lợi, Sài Gòn ward (District 1)
  - Price: Drinks from about 45k VND
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=B%E1%BA%A3n%20C%C3%A0%20Ph%C3%AA%2C%2038/7%20L%C3%AA%20L%E1%BB%A3i%2C%20S%C3%A0i%20G%C3%B2n%20ward%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://znews.vn/5-quan-ca-phe-ngoi-hang-gio-doc-sach-o-tphcm-post1674735.html)

#### Literary events

- **Tết Book Street Festival 2027** (Lễ hội Đường sách Tết Đinh Mùi 2027) · District 1 (Lê Lợi boulevard in 2026; 2027 layout TBA) · $ · *likely open*
  - Saigon's biggest literary event is a week-long open-air book fair around Lunar New Year, with publishers' pavilions, rare-book displays, stage programmes and author meet-ups. The 2026 edition (15–22 Feb) covered more than 13,600 m² across three city sites, sold nearly 84,000 books and drew over a million visits to its central site.
  - Tip: The 2027 dates aren't announced yet. Tết falls on 6 Feb 2027, and the 2026 festival ran from the 28th of the last lunar month to the 6th day of Tết, so expect roughly the first half of February. Tết days 1–3 are the busiest; on day 1, look for the 'lì xì sách' (lucky-money books) giveaways.
  - Hours: 2026: 15–22 Feb; 2027: TBA (around Tết, 6 Feb 2027)
  - Address: 2026 edition: Lê Lợi boulevard, Sài Gòn ward (District 1), with programmes at Nguyễn Văn Bình Book Street; 2027 venue TBA
  - Price: Free entry
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=L%C3%AA%20L%E1%BB%A3i%20Boulevard%2C%20S%C3%A0i%20G%C3%B2n%20ward%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://baovanhoa.vn/xuat-ban/le-hoi-duong-sach-tet-binh-ngo-2026-dat-gan-82-ti-dong-206391.html)
- **Vietnam Poetry Day in Saigon 2027** (Ngày thơ Việt Nam tại TP.HCM 2027) · District 3 (Xuân Hòa ward) · $ · *likely open*
  - The city's public celebration of poetry, held each year around the first full moon of the lunar year. The 2026 edition (27 Feb–1 Mar) ran talks, a 'poetry street', posters of 12 poets tied to the city, 20 tents from local poetry clubs and an exhibition of Vietnamese poems translated into English, French, Chinese, Korean and Japanese.
  - Tip: The translated-poetry exhibition is the easiest way in if you don't read Vietnamese. At the club tents amateur poets recite, so a Vietnamese-speaking friend helps. Watch the HCMC press in early February for the 2027 programme; the full moon falls on 20 Feb 2027.
  - Music: The 2026 edition closed with a concert of poems set to music.
  - Hours: 2026: 27 Feb–1 Mar; 2027: TBA (around 20 Feb 2027)
  - Address: 2026 venue: 81 Trần Quốc Thảo, Xuân Hòa ward (District 3), headquarters of the HCMC Union of Literature & Arts Associations; 2027 venue TBA
  - Price: Public festival; no tickets reported
  - [Google Maps](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=H%E1%BB%99i%20Li%C3%AAn%20hi%E1%BB%87p%20V%C4%83n%20h%E1%BB%8Dc%20Ngh%E1%BB%87%20thu%E1%BA%ADt%20TP.HCM%2C%2081%20Tr%E1%BA%A7n%20Qu%E1%BB%91c%20Th%E1%BA%A3o%2C%20Xu%C3%A2n%20H%C3%B2a%2C%20Ho%20Chi%20Minh%20City) · [Source](https://www.sggp.org.vn/ngay-tho-viet-nam-2026-tai-tphcm-cat-len-tieng-goi-do-thi-moi-post840510.html)

### Saigon: local tips

- Prices: local draught costs 12–50k VND a glass, craft pints roughly 80–140k and signature cocktails at the top bars 260–400k. Hotel and rooftop menus marked '++' add service charge and VAT. Happy hour is the way to save: ~99k cocktails at Layla and Summer Experiment, half price from mid-afternoon to sunset at Social Club, and a free drink if you win BiaCraft's coin toss (5–6pm).
- Walkable bar crawls: on Tôn Thất Đạm, Snuffbox (14, a Gatsby-style speakeasy opened in 2015), Nhau Nhau (89) and BOO SG (119, inside the wet market) are minutes apart. On Lê Thánh Tôn, pair Stir (alley 136) with Nê Saigon (109), the Saigon outpost of Hanoi's phở-cocktail pioneer. On Lý Tự Trọng, Heart of Darkness (31D), Summer Experiment (77–79) and RAW + Atelier (97B), the new bar from Drinking & Healing founder Jimmy Nguyen, are a short walk apart.
- The best cocktail bars hide on the upper floors of scruffy old apartment blocks (Stir, Summer Experiment, Snuffbox): trust the map pin, look for the stairwell or lift, and don't be put off by the entrance. Book Friday and Saturday tables at The Enigma Mansion, Stir and Layla; most bars take reservations and announce guest-bartender nights on Instagram or Facebook.
- Rooftops: sunset falls between about 5:30 and 6:15pm year-round and rail-side tables go first, so book and arrive by 5. Hotel rooftops and the party-led Chill Skybar (26th floor, AB Tower) expect smart casual: no tank tops or flip-flops. In the rainy season (roughly May–November) storms can shut open terraces, so the enclosed EON Heli Bar (52nd floor, Bitexco Financial Tower) and Blank Lounge (floors 75–76, Landmark 81) are the weatherproof views; check Blank Lounge's hours on Google Maps, because its old website now redirects to a gambling site.
- Historic-hotel crawl, about 15 minutes on foot around Lam Sơn Square: start at the Rex Hotel's 5th-floor Garden Bar (20% off drinks 5–7pm; use the 'Rex Hotel Garden Bar' map pin, because an older duplicate shows as closed), catch the 5:45pm cocktail trolley at Saigon Saigon in the Caravelle, move on to M Bar at the Majestic for the river and the 8pm band, and finish with a nightcap at the Park Hyatt's 2 Lam Son.
- Bùi Viện Walking Street is pedestrian-only on weekend evenings and packed from about 8pm. Go once for the spectacle, drink at the plastic-stool beer stalls (draught 20–50k VND), and skip the bucket-and-hookah club bars, where inflated prices and tab disputes happen. Never buy laughing-gas balloons (bóng cười): they're illegal, and an August 2025 police crackdown seized 348 cylinders.
- Nhậu etiquette: beer comes with a bucket of ice, and the table toasts together ('Một, hai, ba, dzô!') and drinks on the toast. Bottles are counted by how many were opened at your table, so leave unopened ones alone and check the count on the bill; street joints are mostly cash-only. When the bars close, 5KU Station (17 Thái Văn Lung) grills and pours until 4am.
- Getting home: Vietnam has zero tolerance for drink-driving, so take Grab or Xanh SM rather than a rented motorbike or an unmetered taxi. Motorbike phone-snatching is the main risk around Bến Thành and Bùi Viện, so keep phones off roadside tables. Since the 2025 ward mergers, addresses may read 'Sài Gòn Ward' or 'Bến Thành Ward' instead of District 1, so search Grab by venue name.
- How to order: cà phê đen đá is iced black, cà phê sữa đá is iced with condensed milk and bạc xỉu is mostly milk with a little coffee. Add 'nóng' for hot and 'ít ngọt' for less sweet. At sock-coffee (vợt) stalls the coffee is brewed ahead and kept hot, so it arrives almost at once.
- Go early for heritage coffee: regulars fill the sock-coffee stalls from about 6:30am, and Cheo Leo closes around 6pm. After dark, the Phan Đình Phùng sock-coffee stall runs 24/7, and Tiệm Cà Phê Linh at 1 Trương Định, by Bến Thành metro station, is open 7am–2am.
- Specialty bars keep daytime hours: MANKI breaks 12:00–13:30 and shuts at 5pm, GRANDMUM shuts at 5pm and Lacàph at 7:30pm, while Every Half stays open until 9:30pm. Book MANKI's seven seats, GRANDMUM's coffee omakase and Lacàph's workshops ahead.
- Taste fine robusta black, as a pour-over or espresso, not just arabica: Every Half's Cư M'gar lot won Gold at the Global Coffee Awards 2025. Buy traceable beans from the roasters as souvenirs, and skip the 'weasel' (cà phê chồn) coffee pushed at souvenir shops, which is often fake or flavoured.
- Coffee crawls: GRANDMUM (alley 82), Hummingbird (103bis), Hoff (170A) and Every Half (232/23) are all on Võ Thị Sáu, and The Workshop (no. 27) shares Ngô Đức Kế with Every Half's Đồng Khởi store (8–24).
- Addresses: since mid-2025 districts have been abolished and wards merged, so listings may say 'Sài Gòn ward' or 'Bến Thành ward' instead of District 1. Search by street and house number. An address like 109/36 Nguyễn Thiện Thuật means house 36 in alley 109; have Grab drop you at the alley mouth and walk in.
- Old apartment blocks such as 42 Nguyễn Huệ and 145 Nguyễn Trãi are still homes: keep your voice down in stairwells and don't photograph residents' doors. At 42 Nguyễn Huệ the lift costs 5k VND per person, refunded when you order, and it's busiest around 8pm.
- Carry small notes: heritage stalls charge roughly 10–35k VND a cup and some take no cards or QR, while apartment and specialty cafés charge roughly 30–120k a drink.
- Find the week's gigs and parties on Saigoneer's events page (saigoneer.com/saigon-events) and RA's Ho Chi Minh City guide (ra.co/guide/vn/hochiminhcity). Most local gigs sell pre-sale tickets through a Google Form in the listing, usually 50–70k VND below the door price. Club nights start around 10pm, fill after midnight and run to 5–6am, and some promoters cut the door to about 100k after 3:30am.
- How you pay for the music: cover-band bars such as Acoustic Bar charge no cover, so you just buy drinks. Jazz rooms and phòng trà add a per-person surcharge (phụ thu) or sell tickets: a cover including one drink at Saxn'Art, 300k–800k VND on an ordinary phòng trà night and 1–2M VND when a star headlines. Local band showcases cost 150–350k VND, often with a drink. Carry cash, as smaller music bars don't always take cards.
- Phòng trà etiquette: book a day or more ahead by phone or Zalo and ask for a central table. Shows run about two hours in Vietnamese with plenty of chat between songs. Pre-1975 song titles in the billing mean bolero/nhạc vàng, and 'nhạc Trịnh' means Trịnh Công Sơn. Dress smart-casual and keep filming to short clips: in May 2026, executives of several live-music brands were prosecuted for posting show recordings on YouTube without permission.
- Opera House planning: Lune's AO Show (18:00, about an hour, mostly weeknights) and HBSO (weekends at 20:00) share the 468-seat house and rarely clash, so you can see AO on a weeknight and HBSO at the weekend. Ignore older guides: Lune's Teh Dar now plays only in Hội An, and Làng Tôi moves to Hanoi from 3 Nov 2026.
- Getting around: Thảo Điền (now An Khánh ward) is the second music hub, 20–30 minutes by Grab from District 1, with Saxn'Art and Blue Monkey on the same street (Nguyễn Văn Hưởng) and Kanpai nearby. Since the 2025 ward mergers, addresses show new ward names, so give drivers the street and number. Grab and Xanh SM run all night, so skip the unmetered taxis touting at closing time. Vietnam's drug laws are severe and police raid clubs, so carry nothing.
- Đờn ca tài tử has no fixed tourist stage in Saigon. Your best bets are Saturday nights at Hai Tô Bar, the Gia Định club's Wednesday-evening sessions at the Bình Thạnh ward traditional house (6B Phan Đăng Lưu), and the weekend riverside buffet at Bình Quới Tourist Village (439k VND including the show). The HCMC History Museum also stages free hát bội (classical Southern opera) on some Sunday mornings, announced a few days ahead on its website.
- More live music: the Caravelle's Saigon Saigon rooftop (reopened 28 Sep 2026; band Mon–Sat 8–10:30pm, Sun 8–10pm) and the Majestic's M Bar both have nightly hotel-rooftop bands. Carmen (8 Lý Tự Trọng; nightly flamenco and Latin house band) and Cội (77–79 Lý Tự Trọng; jazz Thu and Sun from 8:30pm) are long-running but unconfirmed for 2026, so check before going. The free 'No Cover' original-music showcase at Mooney's (Alley 40 Bùi Viện) runs roughly monthly on Saturdays at 7pm, and Le Café des Stagiaires' Thảo Điền rooftop hosts free vinyl-only HCMC Soul Club nights.
- Big dates, Oct 2026–Mar 2027: Sat 17 Oct is a concert pile-up (Anh Trai Vượt Ngàn Chông Gai at The Global City on 17–18 Oct, plus several stadium shows), so expect gridlock and ride surges. In December, watch for the free Saigon Record Fair (6 Dec in 2025), the Saigon Latin Festival (3–7 Dec 2026, in Vũng Tàu), HOZO's riverside festival (27–31 Dec in 2025), NTPMM's year-end show and the Nguyễn Huệ New Year countdown. Trịnh Công Sơn tributes cluster around 28 Feb and 1 Apr. Buy on Ticketbox on release day and avoid Facebook resellers.
- Read before you go: The Quiet American (Greene; set in 1951–52), Dispatches (Michael Herr), The Lover (Duras) and, for the modern city, Nguyễn Phan Quế Mai's Dust Child.
- Fixed literary dates in Oct 2026–Mar 2027: the Tết Book Street Festival (around Tết, 6 Feb 2027) and Vietnam Poetry Day (around the first full moon of the lunar year, 20 Feb 2027). No HCMC Book Fair had been announced for this window as of early Oct 2026, and Vietnam Book & Reading Culture Day (21 April) falls just after it.
- English-language literary nights are small and pop-up. Watch Saigoneer's events calendar for the bilingual Read Poem Together evenings (the 7th was at Ka Coffee, 230G Pasteur, in March 2026), and follow @pagesofpassionvn for talks and workshops.
- Book Street launches and talks cluster at weekends and are mostly in Vietnamese. Its website is stale, so search tuoitre.vn for 'Đường sách' to find the week's programme.
- English books about Vietnam: try Pages of Passion's Vietnam shelf, InBook's translations, and Fahasa's flagship at 40 Nguyễn Huệ (8:00–22:00), whose foreign-language section stocks English histories such as The Illustrated History of Vietnam.
- More reading rooms: the IDECAF library (31 Thái Văn Lung, Sài Gòn ward) for French books and manga, Mon–Fri 7:30–17:00; and, for long stays, Read Station (Nguyễn Thái Bình street, Bảy Hiền ward), a café-library with 10,000+ titles and paid memberships, open 7:00–22:30.
- On the Greene walk, Artbook (43 Đồng Khởi, daily 9:00–18:00) is a gallery-like shop of Phaidon and Taschen art and design books. Browse rather than buy: large-format volumes cost millions of dong.
- Useful words: sách cũ (used books), sách ngoại văn / sách tiếng Anh (foreign-language / English books), sách xưa (old editions), bán theo ký (sold by the kilo). Addresses use the post-2025 ward names: Sài Gòn and Bến Thành wards are in old District 1, Xuân Hòa is in old District 3.
- Gun rules: under Law 42/2024/QH15, air guns, paintball markers and sport bows are 'sports weapons' that only licensed clubs may hold, so shoot only at established clubs like those here, never buy or carry one, and skip airsoft or gel-blaster 'battle' fields (we found none licensed in HCMC). The only live fire open to tourists is the military-run range at the Củ Chi Tunnels.
- Cyber hours are capped: under Decree 147/2024, public game venues may not open from 22:00 to 08:00, so ignore listicles promising '24-hour' gaming cafés; a draft amendment due around November 2026 would move the adult cut-off to midnight and bar under-16s from 21:00. Most other play venues close by 21:30–22:30 too, so game first and drink after.
- Signing up as a foreigner: bring your passport. At Vikings the cashier creates a Vikings ID for you (new accounts get 10 free minutes); full verification for the e-card and points asks for a Vietnamese ID number and phone, and prepaid time stays at the branch where you top up.
- Bring your own logins and pick the right zone: CS2 and PUBG run through Steam, while Valorant accounts are region-locked, so a non-Asia-Pacific account connects to its home servers with high ping. For shooters, pay 14–19k VND an hour for a 360–390Hz Super, Master or Competitive seat rather than the 10k Standard zone's older GPUs.
- Escape rooms (Real Escape, Mind Escape, Master Escape, Spirit Escape, Inside Xcape) are mostly Vietnamese-language horror with live actors: book on Facebook a day ahead, bring four or more players for the bigger rooms and ask whether an English-speaking host can give hints.
- Gaps: we found no walk-in laser-tag arena or PS5/console lounge with 2025–26 evidence, and VR City at SC VivoCity was billed as a six-month run from September 2025. For VR try Timezone Estella Place; for team shooting, paintball at Phú Thọ or the Củ Chi war game.
- Billiards and board games: bi-a halls with pool and Vietnam's beloved 3-cushion carom are on almost every main road and cheap, and HCMC hosted 3-cushion World Cup events in 2023 and 2026. For board games in English, watch Saigoneer's events listings for W.A.L.L. Bar's occasional Wednesday game nights in Thảo Điền (last held 20 May 2026).
- Watching esports: VNGGames packed Rạch Miễu Arena for LoL Worlds final viewing parties in 2023 and 2025, but no 2026 party had been announced by 5 Oct (the final is on 14 Nov at Barclays Center, New York, 12 hours behind Saigon). The Mobile Legends VMC Fall grand final is played live at AEON Mall Tân Phú Celadon on 17 Oct; follow the LMHT, VALORANT and MLBB Vietnam fanpages, as registration fills within hours.
- Ba Lù's two shops are confirmed (Thanh Niên, Mar 2026). The founder's eighth child, Chung Quốc Hùng, runs the original stall in Phùng Hưng market, where the family roasts the next batch around 2pm once the market closes, so arrive well before then. His sister Chung Thị Hoàng, the sixth child, runs the newer shop at the corner of Nguyễn Trãi and Châu Văn Liêm (black 25k, milk coffee 30k, egg coffee 35k VND).
- Phan Đình Phùng's sock coffee really does run through the night: Tuổi Trẻ's reporter found it serving at 4am (14 Sep 2026). Phú Nhuận is on the way to Tân Sơn Nhất airport, so it makes a pre-dawn stop before an early flight; for a sit-down table after midnight, 3 Hào in District 7 stays open until 2:30am.
- At Bar Mitsuboshi, expect a table fee that comes with an otoshi (a small snack), as in Japan: it's the custom, not a tourist surcharge. Mitsuboshi and KURA both close at 11pm and hold only a handful of drinkers, so go early in the evening.
- Don't plan a morning around the songbird café in Tao Đàn Park that older guides and SEO listings still describe: a 2020 report found it had moved out of the park a couple of years earlier, and no 2025–26 report confirms the gathering still happens there.
- How private-room karaoke works: you rent the room by the hour, and the clock starts when you walk in. 2026 market guides put a small room (2–4 people) at roughly 80–150k VND/hour off-peak and 150–300k at peak times (evenings and weekends), with VIP rooms at 1–2M. Beer (by the can or the ice bucket), fruit platters (100–300k) and snacks are billed on top, plus 5–10% service charge and VAT. Agree the rate first, and pass the mic around rather than queueing five songs.
- Avoid hostess karaoke ('karaoke tay vịn' or 'karaoke ôm'). These rooms are staffed with hostesses, bills run into millions of đồng, and some venues front for prostitution. Red flags: women waiting at the door, touts steering you in, no printed price list, staff offering 'girls'. HCMC ordered tighter inspections of karaoke venues in March 2026, so stick to chains such as ICOOL.
- Closing times: on paper karaoke must stop at midnight and dance clubs at 2am (Decree 54/2019), yet ICOOL lists 3am closing and club nights run until 5–6am. Late sessions are normal, but a ward police check can end one without warning.
- Solo but want a private room? ViCo's Karaoke Night at Karaoke Nnice (340 Nguyễn Thị Minh Khai, District 3), on Fri 23 Oct 2026 from 7 to 9:30pm, puts you in a room with members of the ViCo community. It costs 280k VND (200k for members); register through the Google Form on vico.asia.
- Update for Sat 17 Oct: Anh Trai Vượt Ngàn Chông Gai (17–18 Oct), Tinh Hà 'Say Hi' (17–18 Oct) and Phùng Khánh Linh's arena show were all sold out on Ticketbox on 6 Oct 2026. Only Tiffany Young's Edge of Calm Tour (Nguyễn Du Gymnasium, 116 Nguyễn Du, District 1; 18:00; from 1.8M VND) still had tickets.
- Bến Thành Music Lounge (3rd floor, Bến Thành Theatre) has October shows at 20:00–22:00: Trịnh Thăng Bình & Hoàng Tôn (Tue 6), Hoàng Hiệp, Hà Lê & Đinh Mạnh Ninh (Sun 11), Vương Anh Tú & Hoàng Tôn (Fri 23) and Toki Thành Thỏ & K.O (Sat 31). Ticketbox sells seats as surcharges (phụ thu) of 300k–2.2M VND. The 300k seats had sold out for every date by 6 Oct, so the cheapest seat left costs 600k–1M.
- For the grassroots band scene beyond Yoko and Blue Monkey, watch Saigoneer's listings. Úm Ba La (14 Street No. 11, Thảo Điền) puts alt-rock, metalcore and noise bands on one bill at its Scene Incubator nights (#9 was on 20 Sep 2026; 200k VND with a drink). Tone Lab (6 Ngô Thời Nhiệm, next to Acoustic Bar) hosts album launches and the odd free show, booked by Google Form.
- Since the 2025 provincial merger, a 'Ho Chi Minh City' venue on Ticketbox can be on the coast: Bằng Kiều's live show on 24 Oct is at The Grand Hồ Tràm resort, far from the city. Some shows that come up in HCMC searches are in Hanoi, such as Hoàng Tôn, 14 Casper & Bon Nghiêm on 24 Oct and Đinh Mạnh Ninh on 7 Nov. Check the venue line before paying.

### Saigon: closed, moved or doubtful

- **Somme Wine Bar**: Dropped as doubtful: the only evidence is a May 2025 mention with no hours, and researchers rated its status uncertain.
- **Bluish Cafe**: A vinyl listening café that fits the music section better; newest evidence Sep 2025.
- **The Ghé Coffee**: Newest evidence is a July 2024 Saigoneer review; couldn't confirm it is still open.
- **Carmen Bar (8 Lý Tự Trọng)**: No dated 2025–26 evidence found, only older guides; the Google listing (via Wanderlog) isn't flagged closed. Cut in favour of better-documented live bars and mentioned in tips with a check-first warning.
- **Cội Saigon (77–79 Lý Tự Trọng)**: Only listicle evidence (izitour, Jan 2026) for its Thu/Sun jazz nights, and no first-hand 2026 post found. Cut for space and spread (District 1 is already well covered) and mentioned in tips.
- **Broma: Not A Bar (41 Nguyễn Huệ)**: The researchers' closed list says it's permanently closed; the only 2026 mention is a hotel SEO blog.
- **Vừng Ơi Mở Ra (61 Trần Quốc Thảo)**: No first-hand 2025–26 evidence, only listicles repeating older details; researchers flagged it as uncertain.
- **Vina Groove (Bình Thạnh)**: The newest evidence is from January 2024; no hours are published and its website now redirects to Facebook.
- **Meraki Record Store & Art Cafe (Thảo Điền)**: Only its July 2025 soft opening is documented.
- **Phòng trà WE (104 Cao Thắng)**: The newest evidence is from May 2025 and its online listings are patchy.
- **Legato Bar (126 Phạm Viết Chánh)**: The newest evidence is from February 2025, and its own site and Google disagree on band nights.
- **HOZO International Music Festival 2026**: No 2026 edition announced yet (the 2025 edition ran 27–31 Dec at Saigon Riverside Park); mentioned in tips.
- **Saigon Record Fair 2026**: Not yet announced; past editions fell on early-December Saturdays (14 Dec 2024, 6 Dec 2025). Mentioned in tips.
- **Nguyễn Thiện Thuật instrument street**: The newest evidence is from 2017.
- **Emme House (poetry cocktail bar)**: Doubtful: the only evidence is a Feb 2025 Saigoneer feature, nothing found confirms it is still operating in 2026, and it is a cocktail bar rather than a book venue.
- **Read Poem Together (bilingual poetry evenings)**: Doubtful for this window: the volunteer-run series' last listed edition was 17 Mar 2026 at Ka Coffee, and no edition for Oct 2026–Mar 2027 appeared on Saigoneer's events calendar (checked 5 Oct 2026); kept in tips.
- **Cá Chép Book Coffee (researcher tip, 211 Võ Văn Tần)**: Likely closed: Saigoneer's 12 Sep 2026 roundup reports that Cá Chép Bookstore bid farewell to readers in April 2026 after 13 years; the café sat above that bookstore, so the tip was not used.
- **LoL Worlds 2026 Final Viewing Party (VNGGames)**: Doubtful for now: no 2026 viewing party (date, venue or tickets) had been announced as of 5 Oct 2026. VNGGames filled Rạch Miễu Arena for the 2023 and 2025 finals, so it's kept in tips; the 2026 final is on 14 Nov at Barclays Center, New York.
- **VR City (SC VivoCity, District 7)**: Likely closed: the 1,000 m² VR attraction opened on 12 Sep 2025 billed as a six-month run (tickets 299–399k), and research found it missing from SC VivoCity's 2026 tenant directory.
- **Vikings Xô Viết Nghệ Tĩnh (Bình Thạnh)**: Closed: an official Vikings notice said it shut after 24 Jul 2025 pending a move, and it is absent from Vikings' HCMC branch list (checked Oct 2026), though older listicles still recommend it.
- **Vikings Esports Arena Hoàng Văn Thụ**: Closed: the 214-PC arena covered by Thanh Niên in 2023 is absent from Vikings' official HCMC branch list (checked Oct 2026), which now shows only Nguyễn Thị Thập, Lê Quang Định and the two Net Vikings in Thảo Điền and Trần Não.
- **Tao Đàn Park bird café**: Doubtful: a March 2020 travel report (Kathmandu & Beyond) found the bird café had moved out of Tao Đàn Park a couple of years earlier, with keepers meeting on a restaurant terrace across the street, and another guide says it was lost when the park was cut back for the metro. Only an SEO listing (mytour.vn, updated Sep 2026) still places it inside the park, and I found no 2025–26 report of either spot.
- **The Lighthouse**: Round-1 research found it closed in October 2025. The candidate's only evidence, an Aug 2025 Saigoneer listing at 187 Đề Thám, predates that, and no 2026 listing was found.
- **Nguyễn Thiện Thuật Guitar & Instrument Street**: The newest evidence is a May 2024 Tuổi Trẻ report, with no 2025–26 confirmation. It is instrument shopping rather than live music.

### Saigon: considered but not picked

- **2 Lam Son (Park Hyatt Saigon)**: Not selected: a polished hotel cocktail bar on Tatler's Best 20 Bars of Vietnam 2026, but the hotel slots went to bars with views and history; suggested in tips as the nightcap on the historic-hotel crawl.
- **5KU Station**: Not selected: a rowdy grill-and-beer joint whose main appeal is opening until 4am; kept in tips as a late-night option.
- **86 Proof Whiskey Bar**: Not selected: a single 2025 source and nothing standout beyond its happy hour; Thảo Điền is covered by BiaCraft and Lửa.
- **Blank Lounge (Landmark 81)**: Not selected: the highest view in town, but a café-lounge with only 'likely open' evidence, and its old domain now redirects to a gambling site; mentioned in tips as a weatherproof view.
- **BOO SG Cocktail Bar**: Not selected for space (newest evidence Nov 2025); mentioned in the Tôn Thất Đạm crawl tip.
- **Bui Vien Walking Street**: Not selected: still operating (Sept 2026 guide), but a loud backpacker party strip rather than a drinks destination; covered in tips with pricing and safety advice.
- **Chang Vang Rooftop**: Not selected: newest evidence is from January 2025, and the hotel rooftops offer better drinks and views.
- **Chill Skybar (AB Tower)**: Not selected: a DJ-led party sky bar more than a drinks destination; mentioned in tips for its dress code.
- **Drinking & Healing**: Not selected: No.99 on Asia's 50 Best Bars 2024, but founder Jimmy Nguyen left in 2025 and its listed opening days conflict; his new RAW + Atelier is mentioned in tips.
- **East West Brewing Co.**: Not selected for space: the craft-beer slots went to Heart of Darkness, Pasteur Street and BiaCraft.
- **EON Heli Bar (Bitexco Financial Tower)**: Not selected: newest evidence is from September 2025; mentioned in tips as the enclosed rainy-season view.
- **Hai Bar (Sheraton Saigon, Level 23)**: Not selected: the former Level 23 Wine Bar only reopened after a 2026 renovation, and no reviews of the new concept were found.
- **Hybrid Saigon**: Not selected for space: a strong experimental bar on Tatler's Best 20 Bars of Vietnam 2026, but the downtown cocktail picks already cover the style.
- **MAD Wine Bar & Eatery**: Not selected: more a restaurant than a bar; mentioned in the Lửa tip as a nearby alternative.
- **Ne Saigon Cocktail Bar**: Not selected: a promising 2026 newcomer that is only months old; mentioned in tips as a stop alongside Stir.
- **Quan Nhau Xom**: Not selected: a single 2026 listing and nothing that sets it apart from the nhậu picks.
- **RAW + Atelier**: Not selected for space (on Tatler's Best 20 Bars of Vietnam 2026); mentioned in the Lý Tự Trọng crawl tip.
- **Rex Rooftop Garden Bar (Rex Hotel)**: Not selected: low-rise and pricey for what it is, and Saigon Saigon tells the war-era story better; included in the historic-hotel crawl tip.
- **Snuffbox**: Not selected for space (newest evidence Nov 2025); mentioned in the Tôn Thất Đạm crawl tip.
- **Thirty 59**: Not selected for space; mentioned in the Lửa tip as a nearby small-producer wine stop.
- **Vertical Sky Bar (Liberty Central Saigon Riverside)**: Not selected: the river-view slot went to M Bar, and its draw is the live bands more than the drinks.
- **14 Tôn Thất Đạm apartment cafés**: Tenant list dates from 2023 and couldn't be reconfirmed; the block is now best known for the Snuffbox speakeasy (a bar). 42 Nguyễn Huệ and 145 Nguyễn Trãi cover the café-apartment experience better.
- **A Cafe Specialty Coffee**: Cut for space: listed by The Dot (Mar 2026), but no hours or prices found and the specialty picks already cover house roasting.
- **BẢN Cà Phê**: Cut for space: a pleasant hidden book café near the Opera House, but less distinctive than the picks.
- **Bosgaurus Coffee Roasters**: Cut for space: a strong Vietnamese-arabica roaster (The Dot, Mar 2026), but the specialty slots went to more distinctive experiences.
- **Du Miên Garden Café**: Cut for space: far from the centre in Gò Vấp, and a big garden venue rather than a coffee destination.
- **Hoff Coffee Brewers**: Cut for space; no hours or prices found. Listed by The Dot (Mar 2026), so it is named in the Võ Thị Sáu crawl tip.
- **The Hummingbird Café & Roastery**: Cut for space; hours only from an undated guide. Listed by The Dot (Mar 2026), so it is named in the Võ Thị Sáu crawl tip.
- **Là Việt Coffee**: Conflicting Saigon addresses: The Dot (Mar 2026) gives 57A Tú Xương and 191 Hai Bà Trưng, while the brand's own 2026 list names only 72/21 Trần Quốc Toản and 60 Phó Đức Chính. Too uncertain to send readers.
- **Tiệm Cà Phê Linh**: Cut for space: a newly built nostalgia café; named in the tips as a late-night option (7am–2am, Feb 2026).
- **Passengers**: Cut for space: a niche cat café with no hours or prices found.
- **Phường Cà Phê**: Cut for space: a niche railway-side alley café with no hours or prices found.
- **RedDoor**: Cut for space; the Đa Kao branch keeps weekday daytime hours only and the specialty picks are stronger.
- **Saigon Book Street (Phương Nam Book Coffee)**: A bookshop street more than a coffee destination.
- **Thương Rooftop**: Cut for space: a rooftop sunset café whose newest evidence is Apr 2025; the river cafés cover sunsets.
- **Tokyo Moon Cafe**: A Korean-tea café rather than a coffee stop.
- **Cà phê vợt Chú Thanh**: Far out in District 11 with conflicting addresses (313 vs 478–480 Tân Phước); Cheo Leo, Ba Lù and Phan Đình Phùng cover sock coffee.
- **XLIII Coffee**: Cut for space: a rare-lot specialty flagship (The Dot, Mar 2026) that overlaps with MANKI and The Workshop.
- **Le Café des Stagiaires (Thảo Điền)**: Not selected: its music is occasional (HCMC Soul Club vinyl nights, Vol. 2 on 22 Aug 2026, and some RA-listed parties) rather than a regular programme, and Luu Bar and The Observatory cover DJ culture better. Mentioned in tips.
- **Artbook (Tiệm sách Artbook)**: Not selected: a gallery-like art-book shop at 43 Đồng Khởi (Znews, Sep 2026), but its large-format imports cost millions of dong and suit design fans more than readers; mentioned in tips as a stop on the Greene walk.
- **Doxa Cafe**: Not selected: a 4,000-book café-library in An Khánh ward (Znews, Aug 2026), but it is daytime-only (7:00–17:00) and far from the centre; Bản Cà Phê covers book cafés in District 1.
- **Fahasa Nguyễn Huệ Bookstore**: Not selected: a handy chain flagship open till 22:00 on the walking street, but Pages of Passion and InBook are better English-language shops; its English books on Vietnam are mentioned in tips.
- **Huỳnh Thủy Lê Ancient House (The Lover's house), Sa Đéc**: Not selected as a separate pick: it is a day trip to Đồng Tháp, so it is folded into Marguerite Duras's Saigon as the finale (now a national heritage site; Tuổi Trẻ, Dec 2025).
- **Phương Nam Book City (former Khải Hoàn cinema)**: Not selected: a 1,500 m² chain store with mostly Vietnamese stock, and the English-language shops serve this traveller better. Saigoneer's Sept 2026 roundup also mentions PNC (Phương Nam's operator) announcing the closure of a 44-year-old store this year, so check branches before going.
- **Rex Hotel Rooftop Garden**: Not selected as a separate pick: its 'Five O'Clock Follies' history is folded into the Quiet American walk, and bar hours and prices aren't published.
- **Trần Huy Liệu Used-Book Strip (Tổng Hợp 160 & Momo)**: Not selected: rising rents have closed most of the street's shops, the newest evidence is from Aug 2025 with older hours, and Trần Nhân Tôn is the stronger used-book street.
- **Visa VMC Fall 2026 Grand Final (Mobile Legends)**: Not selected: a confirmed live final (17 Oct 2026, AEON Mall Tân Phú Celadon, 500 million VND prize pool), but Mobile Legends isn't a shooter and ticketing wasn't published; kept in tips.
- **W.A.L.L. Bar Saigon (board-game nights)**: Not selected: the hosted English-language board-game nights are irregular, with only two listed in 2026 (11 Mar and 20 May) and none since on Saigoneer's events calendar (checked 5 Oct 2026); kept in tips.
- **ParTee Saigon**: Not selected: a breezy riverside mini-golf course and driving range in Nhà Bè (Saigoneer, Dec 2025), but it now leans towards corporate events, open-play slots need checking ahead, and it sits outside this traveller's shooter focus.
- **Chill Skybar**: Not selected: already named in the guide's rooftop tip (26th floor, AB Tower, dress code); a DJ-led party sky bar with bottle-service tables rather than a drinks destination.
- **Cây Si Quán (Hanoi-style bia hơi)**: Not selected: a single 2025 source (vinwonders) with no hours or prices, and Saigon's own nhậu culture is already covered by Ốc Oanh and the Hoàng Sa canal strip.
- **Bar Haus**: Not selected: a likeable 2025 neighbourhood bar (The Dot, Jan 2026), but no hours or prices are published, and Nhau Nhau already covers a cheaper cocktail option.
- **Motly**: Not selected: a 2025 bia hơi makeover (The Dot, Jan 2026) with no published hours or prices; Thảo Điền is already covered by BiaCraft, Pasteur Street and Lửa.
- **Fin Saigon Cafe**: Not selected: a pleasant specialty café inside the Phan Xích Long apartment estate, but the newest evidence is a March 2025 listing and the specialty and café-apartment picks are stronger.
- **Haru Craft (formerly Haru Cottage)**: Not selected: open in January 2026 (Saigoneer), but its draw is the ceramics workshops rather than the coffee, and no hours or prices are published.
- **Mão A Chai**: Not selected: a calm chai and Thái Nguyên tea room upstairs at 26 Lý Tự Trọng rather than a coffee stop; the newest evidence is Saigoneer, April 2025.
- **Thiên Cảnh Viên (formerly Nọong Ơi)**: Not selected: it reopened on 17 Aug 2026 after a fire (VnExpress), but it's a spectacle garden café where the setting, not the coffee, is the draw, far out in Thủ Đức, with a reported 130k VND entry ticket and no street address in the research.
- **Anh Trai Vượt Ngàn Chông Gai 2026 – Concert Day 1 & 2**: Sold out: Ticketbox shows every tier for both nights (17–18 Oct 2026, The Global City) sold out on 6 Oct 2026. Covered in tips.
- **Tinh Hà "Say Hi" Concert (HCMC nights)**: Sold out: both Saigon nights (17–18 Oct 2026, Vạn Phúc City) are sold out in every tier on Ticketbox (6 Oct 2026). Covered in tips.
- **Phùng Khánh Linh – Giữa Một Vạn Tour (Mở Rộng), HCMC chapter**: Sold out: every Ticketbox tier for 17 Oct 2026 at Phú Thọ Indoor Arena (700k–12M VND) showed sold out on 6 Oct 2026.
- **HBSO: A Night of Virtuoso Concertos**: Not selected: already in the guide, with programme and date (Sat 10 Oct 2026), in the HBSO pick.
- **General Levy Live in Saigon @ Blue Monkey**: Not selected: already in the guide's Blue Monkey pick (9 Oct 2026), and no door price has been published.
- **No Cover @ Mooney's Irish Pub**: Not selected: already in the guide's tips as a free, roughly monthly Saturday showcase; the latest listing found is the 2-year anniversary show on 27 Jun 2026.
- **Karaoke Nnice (Nguyễn Thị Minh Khai flagship)**: Not selected: ICOOL covers private-room karaoke with later hours, and Nnice publishes no room rates. ViCo's 23 Oct community karaoke night at Nnice (340 Nguyễn Thị Minh Khai) is in tips.
- **Úm Ba La**: Not selected: irregular programme, with four Saigoneer listings from 2025 up to Scene Incubator #9 on 20 Sep 2026, and Blue Monkey and Yoko already cover the band scene. Mentioned in tips.
- **Tone Lab**: Not selected: only two 2026 gigs are listed on Saigoneer (Bedlam Royals album launch; VAT Band, 1 Aug 2026), with no regular programme. It is already mentioned in the Acoustic Bar tip, and in tips.
- **Apocalypse Now**: Not selected: a commercial late-night party club rather than a music destination. The newest first-hand listing is Hem Homies Fest (Sep 2025), and The Observatory and Luu Club already cover dancing after midnight.
- **Cine Saigon**: The newest first-hand listing is Gãy #21 (Aug 2025), and a 2026 nightlife guide warns of hidden service fees. Not enough to recommend.
- **Mor.Stereo**: Not selected: only one 2026 party listing (29th Floor Radio's anniversary, Apr 2026). It books event by event and has no regular nights.
